slain therein with a great body of his soldiers; and Tula ( ?) burned Aman (?)
of Dún Ollaig.


Talorgg, son of Acithaen, and Domnall Brec son of Eochu, died.


The killing of Rothachtach, and of Dar Gart son of Finngaine.


Death of Banbán  Oscach (?) the learned.



# 687 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 686.


The killing of Feradach son of Congal.


Repose of Do-Chuma Conoc, abbot of Glenn dá Locha.


The falling asleep of Roiséne, abbot of Corcach Mór.


Death of Oiséne, bishop of the monastery of Finntan son of Finnguine.


Adamnán brought back sixty former captives to Ireland.

# 688 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 687.


Repose of Ségéne from Achad Claidib, bishop of Ard Macha.


The slaying of Canu son of Gartnaid.


Fínnechta assumed clerical life.


The battle of Imlech Pich in which fell Dub da Inber, king of Ard Cianachta,
and Uarchride, descendant of Oiséne; and Congalach son of Conaing escaped by
flight. Niall, son of Cernach, was victor.

    (Gabairchenn recited:

	Sad are the Conaille today,
	Fittingly indeed after the death of Uarchride;
	A smile will come none too readily
	In Ard after the death of Dub da Inber.
	Wistful
	Is the grief which afflicts Tadc's land
	Without Dub Cúile, without Bran's son,
	Without Dub dá Inber in Ard.
	Sorrowful
	Are your glances at their grave-stones,
	With your hounds, your hunting-dogs, your women
	In the possession of your enemies.
	If Crunnmael's son should fail
	To requite me thus for my yearning
	My tears for the dead of Imblech
	Would be tears of blood and gore.)



# 689 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 688.


Iolán, bishop of Cenn Garad, died.


Fínnechta returns to the kingship.


The killing of Diarmait of Mide, son of Airmedach Caech.


Death of Cathusach, grandson of Domnall Brec.


Death of Do-Chinne of Daire Brugais.


Death of Feradach son of Tuathalán.


Death of Mael Dúin son of Conall Crannanma.


Part of the sun was obscured.

# 690 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 689.


Congal son of Mael Dúin son of Aed Bennán, king of Iarmumu, and Duinechaid son

The burning of Ard Macha.


Death of Finnguine the Tall and Feradach Méth son of Nechtlec; and Coblaith,

Justinian the Younger, son of Constantine, reigned 10 years.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 690.

The Dál Riata despoiled the Cruithin and the Ulaid.


Fidchellach son of Flann, king of Uí Maine, dies.


A great windstorm on the sixteenth of the Kalends of October 16 Sept. caused
the drowning of some six of the community of Í.

# 692 A.D.

Kalends of January second feria, seventh of the moon. AD 691.


Adamnán sets out for Ireland in the 14th year after the repose of Failbe.


Bishop Conchad dies.




Fergus son of Aedán, king of the Province of Ulster, died.


The killing of Mael Díthraib son of Eóganán.


The moon turned to the colour of blood on the Feast of the Nativity of St
Martin.


The siege of Dún Deauae Dibsi (?).

Kalends of January

. AD 692.


Bruide son of Bile, king of Foirtriu, dies, and Alphin son of Nechtan.

Cellach son of Rónán Mór dies.


A battle between the Osraige and the Laigin in which Faelchar grandson of Mael
Odor fell.


The killing of Ainfthech and Nia Néill and the sons of Boendo.


Death of Dar Gart son of Finnguine.


A battle against Penda.

# 694 A.D.

. AD 693.

# 695 A.D.

Kalends of January sixth feria, tenth of the moon. AD 694.

by Aed son of Dlúthach son of Ailill son of Aed Sláine, and by Congalach son of
Conaing son of Congal son of Aed Sláine.


    Mo-Ling of Luachair recited:

	Alas for Fínnechta,
	Today he lies in a gory bed;
	May he have among the men of heaven
	reward for remitting the cattle-tribute.
	The gap in which Fínnechta was smitten,
	Swift kings were laying one another low;
	Aed the swift, though he encounter(?) surliness,
	When he has cast them down(?), he does not ride over them(?).


The slaying of Tadc son of Failbe, in Glenn Geimin.


Repose of Menn Bairenn, abbot of Achad Bó.


Gaimide of Lugmad fell asleep.


Death of Bran son of Conall Bec.


Tomnat wife of Ferchar dies.


Death of Conall son of Tuathal.

# 696 A.D.

. AD 695.

Cuiméne of Mugdorna rests.

. AD 701.

# 703 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 702.


The battle of Mag Cuilinn in Ard Ua nEchdach between the Ulaid and the Britons,
in which Radgann's son, an enemy of God's churches, fell. The Ulaid were
victors.


The battle of Corann in which fell the king of Ireland, Loingsech son of Aengus
son of Domnall son of Aed son of Ainmire, i.e. by Cellach of Loch Cime son of
Ragallach, together with his three sons, and two sons of Colgu, and Dub Díberg
son of Dúngal and Fergus Forcraid and Congal of Gabar, and many other leaders.
On Saturday, the fourth of the Ides of July, at the sixth hour, this battle was
fought. The battle of Corann, in which fell Loingsech son of Aengus, king of
Ireland, with his three sons, and also the king of Cairpre Droma Cliab, the
king of Uí Chonaill Gabra, and ten other Irish kings. The battle was fought at
Claenfhinn in Óenach Loga situate between Cenél Conaill and Connachta. Cellach
of Loch Cime son of Ragallach son of Uata, king of Connacht, and . . .

fell two sons of Cellaeh of Cuala,


Fiachra and Fiannamail, and Luirg with Cellach's Britons; and shortly
afterwards Cairpre, son of Cú Cholainn, was killed.


Mael Doborchon, bishop of Cell Dara, rested.


A battle gained over the Orkneymen, in which Artablair's son fell.


A pestilence called bacach with dysentery in Ireland.


Macnia son of Dub dá Inber dies.


The killing of Conall son of Feradach.

# 710 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 709.


Conamail son of Failbe, abbot of Í, rests.


The burning of Cell Dara.


Congal son of Fergus of Fána son of Conall of Gulbu i.e. Congal of Cenn Magair
king of Temair died suddenly i.e. of a fit.


A conflict among the Cenél Comgaill in which two sons of Nechtan son of Dar
Garta were killed.


Aengus son of Mael Anfaid was killed in Sci.


Fiachra son of Dúngal was slain among the Cruithin.


Colmán son of Sechnusach, abbot of Lothra, dies.


Justinianus II, with his son Tiberius, reigned 6 years.


Fergal son of Mael Dúin begins to reign.

# 711 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 710.


Faelán grandson of Silne dies.


The battle of the Uí Méith in which Tnuthach son of Mochloinges, king of Uí
Méith, and Cú Raí son of Aed son of Dlúthach fell.


A slaughter of the Picts by the Saxons in Mag Manonn, where Finnguine son of
Deile Roith met an untimely death.


Cenn Faelad abbot of Fobar dies.


An encounter of Britons and the Dál Riata at Lorg Ecclet, in which the Britons
were defeated.


Murgal son of Nóe dies.


Overthrow of the Uí Neill at Cuinche Robairgi, in which the son of Condi and
the sons of Dibcheéne fell.


Dicolan the learned, and Ultán moccu Min, bishop of Telach Olann, died.



# 712 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 711.


Coeddi, bishop of Í, rests.


The burning of Tairpert Boiter.


Bede composed a great book this year.


Congas son of Dar Garto dies.


The siege of Aberte by Selbach.


A battle between two descendants of Aed Sláine, in which Maine son of Niall was
killed. Flann son of Aed son of Dlúthach was victor.


The Ulaid were overthrown and Dubthach son of Béc of Bairche fell therein.


Two sons of Feradach son of Mael Dúin perished in a slaughter of the Cenél
Laegaire.


A battle between the Laigin Desgabair, in which Bran grandson of Mael Dúin and
his sons fell.

U712.10

Dubguala, abbot of Glenn dá Locha, died.


Dluthach son of Fidchellach is burned by fire.

# 713 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 712.


Baetán, bishop of Inis Bó Finne, died.


Fáilbe Bec, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, rests.


Oswy's daughters dies in the monastery of St Hilda.


Cinaed son of Derile and Mathgernan's son were killed.


Doirbéne obtained the abbacy of Í, and after five months in the primacy he died
on Saturday, the fifth of the Kalends of November 28 Oct..


Cormac son of Ailill, king of Mumu, was killed in battle.


Tolarg son of Drostan is held captive by his brother, king Nechtan.


Cú Cherca, king of Osraige, dies.


Sechnasach, king of Uí Maine, died.



# 714 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 713.


A battle of varying fortunes in Mag Singitae at Bile Teined in Asal, where
Flann son of Aed son of Dlúthach and Dub Dúin grandson of Béc fell in the
second encounter of the battle, and Colgu and Aed son of Diarmait were slain in
the first.


Dún Ollaig is constructed by Selbach.


Ailén Daingen is destroyed.


Fogartach grandson of Cernach was expelled from the kingship. He went to
Britain.


An overthrow i.e. of Garbshalach in Mide in which Forbasach grandson of Congal,
king of Uí Fhailgi, fell by the men of Mide on the same day as the battle
mentioned above.


Great drought.


A battle between two sons of Béc of Bairche and Bresal's son, king of Uí
Echach, and the victors therein were Béc's sons.


This year pilgrims, i.e. In Clárainech with all his community, were killed by
the Munstermen.


A bright night in autumn.

# 715 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 714.


Cellach of Cualu, king of Laigin, Flann Febla son of Scannlán of the Uí Méith,
abbot of Ard Macha, and bishop Cilléne, abbot of Ferna, died.


The killing of Murchad son of Diarmaid son of Airmedach Caech, king of Uí
Néill.


Aed Dub, king of Uí Fhidgeinti, Flaithnia son of Colgu, the learned, and
Mo-Chonna of Cuerni ( ?), fell asleep.


The hosting by Murchad son of Bran to Cashel.

# 716 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 715.


The killing of the king of the Saxons, Osred, son of Aldfrid, grandson of Oswy.


Garnat son of Deile Roith dies.


Fogartach grandson of Cernach reigns again.




The date of Easter is changed in the monastery of Í.


Faelchú, son of Doirbéne, in the 74th year of his age assumed the see of
Columba on Saturday the fourth of the Kalends of September 29 Aug.


Death of Céile Tigernaig, abbot of Cluain Eóis.


Flann Foirbthe son of Fogartach died.


Death of Artbran son of Mael Dúin.

# 717 A.D.

Kalends of January, AD 716.


Dúnchad son of Cenn Faelad, abbot of Í, died.


Etulb son of Eculb died.


Condalach son of Conaing, king of Uí Chremthainn, was killed.


Expulsion of the community of Í beyond Dorsum Brittaniae by king Nechtan.


An encounter between the Dál Riata and Britons at the rock called Minuirc, and
the Britons were defeated.


Disturbance of the fair of Tailtiu by Fogartach, in which Ruba's son and Dub
Sléibe's son fell.

# 718 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 717.


Cuthwine's son, king of the Saxons, dies.


Béc of Bairche dies.


The battle of Cenannas, in which fell Tuathal grandson of Faelchú and Cellach
Diathraibh and Gormgal son of Aed son of Dlúthach and Amalgaid grandson of
Conaing and his brother Fergal. Conall Grant was victor, and this Conall Grant,
grandson of Cernach, was killed two months after the battle by Fergal, son of
Mael Dúin.


Crónán grandson of Eóán, abbot of Les Mór, dies.


Fiannamail grandson of Bógaine son of Finn, superior of Inis Maige Sam, and
bishop Dub Dúin grandson of Faelán, abbot of Cluain Iraird, died.


Conrí, son of Congal Cennfhata, and Ailill son of Fínnechta, were killed.


A shower of honey rained upon Othan Bec, a shower of blood upon the foss of
Laigin. Hence Niall Frosach who was born at that time, is so named.


An eclipse of the moon at its full.



# 719 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 718.


Airmedach son of Tadc and Críchán king of Uí Meicc Uais were killed, and
Erthuile son of Fergus Goll was killed.


Drostán of Derthach rested in Ard Brecán.


Cú Dimergo dies.


Tuibride grandson of Dúnchad was killed.


An encounter among the Laigin i.e. the battle of Finnabair, in which Aed son of
Cellach fell.


The battle of Finnglen between two sons of Ferchar the Tall, in which
Ainfcellach was slain, on Thursday, the sixth of the Ides of September.


The sea-battle of Ard Nesbi between Dúnchad Bec with the Cenél Gabráin and
Selbach with the Cenél Loairn, and Selbach was repulsed, on Friday, the day
before the Nones 6th of September or October, and a number of nobles fell
therein.


The killing in Loch Uaithne of the two lords, i.e. two sons of Mael Fothartaig,
with their kinsman, namely Cremthann Corrach.


The killing of the community of Suibne in Ard Macha.

U719.10

A dry summer.

# 720 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 719.


A rainy summer.


Sínach of Inis Clothrann fell asleep.


A great sea-flood in the month of October.


Theodore reigned one year.

# 721 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 720.


Dúnchad Bec, king of Cenn Tíre, dies.


An earthquake in October.


A battle between the Connachta and Corco Baiscinn, in which Talamnach's son
fell.


The killing of Cú Dínaisc, son of Rothechtach.


Death of Maenach, abbot of Lann Léire.


The wasting of Mag Breg by Cathal son of Finnguine, and by Murchad son of Bran.




Death of Cuanna of Ros Eó.


An invasion of the Laigin by Fergal, and the cattletribute was imposed and the
hostages of the Laigin secured for Fergal son of Mael Dúin.


Inmesach the religious established a law with the peace of Christ over the
island of Ireland.

# 722 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 721.


Mael Ruba died in Apor Crossan in the 80th year of his age.


Colgu, king of Ard Ladrann, was killed.


Mael Corgais from Druim Ing, and Bile son of Eilphín, king of Ail Cluaithe,
die.


Fer da Crích son of Congalach died.


Cuanán of Cell Deilge, and Derir of Daiminis grandson of Colla, Cuana of Druim
Cuilinn, Cilléne of Loch Gerg, die.


Feidilmid assumed the office of abbot of Í.


Faelán of Martarthech, Sidal of Druim Laidgnin, died.


The battle of Almain on the third of the Ides 11th of December, the sixth
feria, in which fell Fergal son of Mael Dúin, son of Mael Fithrich son of Aed
Uairidnach, i.e. by Murchadh son of Bran; and Conall Menn king of Cenél
Cairpri; Clothgna son of Colgu; Dub dá Crích; Flann son of Rogallach; Aed
Laigen son of Fithchellach, king of Uí Maine; the sons of Muirgius; Nuada son
of Dúnchad; Éicnech son of Colgu, king of Airthir; Fergal grandson of
Aithechda.

# 723 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 722.


The burning of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


Death of Ailchú of Mainister Buiti.


Indrechtach son of Muiredach, king of Connacht, dies.


The entry of Selbach into clerical life.


Sínach of Tailtiu dies.



# 724 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 723.


Faelchú son of Doirbéne, abbot of Í, fell asleep. Cilléne the Tall succeeded
him as abbot of Í.


Cú Chongalt grandson of Cú Melde, Murdobur of Granasc, die.


The battle of Cenn Deilgden in which fell Fogartach grandson of Cernach i.e.
son of Niall son of Cernach Sotal son of Diarmait grandson of Aed Sláine.
Cinaed son of Írgalach was victor.


Cuinnles abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis died.


The killing of Lethaithech son of Cú Charat.


Caech Scuili, scribe of Daire Calgaig, rested.

# 725 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
.AD 724.


Cillenéne grandson of Colla, abbot of Othain, Aldchú of Dam Liac, die.


The fortified island of Crach's son is constructed.


Simul son of Drust is imprisoned.


Colmán Uamach, scribe of Ard Macha, Ruben son of Conna, scribe of Mumu,
Broccán's son from Tech Taille, who was a good master in expounding Christ's
Gospel, and Colmán of Banbán, scribe of Cell Dara all fell asleep.


Death of Bran Muimnech and Cas of Coba.


A dark and blood-red moon on the eighteenth of the Kalends of January.


Congal son of Mael Anfaid, Brec of Foirtriu, Oan abbot of Eig, die.

# 726 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 725.


Nectan son of Derile is imprisoned by king Drust.


Do-Chonna the devout, bishop of Condaire, dies.


Tolargan Maphan dies.


The killing at an immature age of Crimthann son of Cellach in the battle of
Belach Lice.


Repose of Mainchéne of Lethglenn.


The killing of Ailill son of Bodbchad of Mide.



# 727 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 726.


Death of Ailchú, abbot of Cluain Iraird.


The battle of Druim Fornocht between Cenél Conall and Cenél Eógain, in which
Flann son of Aurthuile and Snédgus Derg, descendant of Mrachide, were killed.


The encounter of Irros Foichnae between Selbach and the 'family' of Echaid,
grandson of Domnall, in which some of the Airgialla fell.


Conall son of Moudán was crowned with martyrdom.


The relics of Adomnán are brought over to Ireland and the law is promulgated
anew.


The battle of Móin between the two Laigin, in which Laidgnén son of Cú Meldae
fell. Dúnchad was victor.


Murchad son of Bran, king of Laigin, dies.


Dub dá Inber son of Congalach, king of the Cruithin, was killed.


The battle of Bairenn or Inis Bregainn, in which Eterscél son of Cellach of
Cualu, and Congal son of Bran fell. Faelán was victor.

U727.10

The falling asleep of Céile Críst.

# 728 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 727.


The battle of Druim Corcain between Flaithbertach son of Loingsech and Cinaed
son of Írgalach son of Conaing Cumach son of Congalach son of Aed Sláine, in
which Cinaed, and Eudus son of Ailill, Mael Dúin son of Feradach, and Dúnchad
son of Cormac fell.


The battle of Ailenn between two brothers, the sons of Murchad son of Bran; and
Dúnchad the elder having been killed, Faelán, the younger, reigns.


Flann of Aentrab, abbot of Bennchor, died.


The battle of Monidhcrobh between the Picts themselves, in which Aengus was
victor, and many were slain on the side of king Eilpín. A woeful battle was
fought between the same parties near Caissel Créidi, where Eilpín was put to
flight.


Domnall son of Cellach, king of Connacht, dies.


Repose of Mac Bethach, a learned Munsterman.



# 729 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 728.


Egbert, a soldier of Christ, rests on the second day of Easter.


The battle of Monid Carno near Loch Laegde between the hosts of Nectan and the
army of Aengus, and Nectan's exactors fell i.e. Biceot son of Monet, and his
son, Finnguine son of Drostan, Feroth son of Finnguine, and many others; and
the adherents of Aengus were triumphant.


The battle of Druim Derg Blathug in the territory of the Picts between Aengus
and Drust, king of the Picts, and Drust fell.


The killing of Cathal Corc, son of Niall.

# 730 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 729.


An earthquake on the sixth of the Ides 8th of February, the fourth feria
Wednesday.


Bede became famous.


The return of the relics of Adamnán from Ireland in the month of October.


Bran, son of Eógan, Selbach, son of Ferchar, died.


Suibne alias son of Crunnmael, grandson of Mruichesach, bishop of Ard Macha,
Mac Onchon, scribe of Cell Dara, the Gaul from Lilcach, and the son of Cú
Chumba, scribe of Cluain Moccu Nóis, fell asleep.


Oitechde son of Baithectde, Blamac's son, and Aengus son of Béc of Bairche,
rested.


The killing of the son of Cinadon.


A camp mêlée against Domnall son of Murchad in Cúla i.e. adaigh noidhe nephain,
or of Imlech Senaich.


Cochall Odar, scribe of the community of Bennchor, fell asleep.

U730.10

The battle of Fernmag in which Cétomun fell.

# 731 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 730.


The burning of Cúl Raithin.


Echaid's entry into clerical life.


Cuthwine's son, king of the Saxons, is imprisoned.


The burning of Tairpert Boitir by Dúngal.


A battle between the Cruithin and the Dál Riata of In Muirbolg, in which the
Cruithin were defeated.




A battle between the son of Aengus and the son of Congus, but Bruide defeated
Talorgan, who was put to flight.


Donngal son of Congal son of Fergus dies.


Faeldobur Bec the learned, of Fobar, Adamnán bishop of Ráith Maige Aenaig, and
Colmán grandson of Lítán, doctor of religion, rested.


The killing of Maenach son of Sechnasach.

U731.10

Death of Echaid son of Colgu, anchorite of Ard Macha.


Colmán of Telach Ualann and Brec of Berba fell asleep.


Coblaith daughter of Cellach of Cualu dies.

# 732 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 731.


Death of Flann Sinna grandson of Colla, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


The killing of Daithgus son of Baeth, king of the Déisi.


Dub dá Lethe son of Dúnchú, and Flann of Cuirrech son of Aithechda, die.


The battle of Connacht, in which Muiredach son of Indrechtach fell.


The pontiff of Mag Eó na Saxan, Gerald, died.


Magnus, grandson of Míthrebtha, philosopher of Ireland, died.


Cellach daughter of Dúnchad of the Uí Liathain, a most excellent and gracious
queen, fell asleep.


Teimnén of Cell Garad, a devout cleric, rested.


Cellach son of Tuathal, king of Uí Chremthainn, was killed.

U732.10

A battle between Cenél Conaill and Cenél Eógain, in which Aed son of Fergal
triumphed over Flaithbertach son of Loingsech son of Aengus son of Domnall son
of Aed son of Ainmire of whose force these leaders were killed: Flann Gohan son
of Congal son of Fergus, Flaithgus son of Dub Díberg.


Tomaltach son of Duinechaid dies.


A battle between the Laigin Desgabair and the Munstermen, in which Aed son of
Colgu was victor.


Sebdann daughter of Corc, abbess of Cell Dara, died.


Fergus son of Conall Oircnech, and Ferdomnach, scribe of Ard Macha, died.


Congalach of Cnucha dies.



# 733 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 732.


Dúngal son of Selbach profaned Torach when he forcibly removed Bruide from it,
and on the same occasion he invaded Inis Cuirenrigi.


Muiredach son of Ainfcellach assumed the kingship of Cenél Loairn.


Another encounter between Aed son of Fergal and the Cenél Conaill in Mag Itha
in which Conaing son of Congal son of Fergus and many others fell.


Birth of Donnchad son of Domnall.


The slaying of Aed son of Conac, king of Irluachair.


The slaying of Echaid of Coba, son of Bresal.


The overthrow of Cathal by Domnall in Tailtiu, and the overthrow of Fallomun by
Cathal in Tlachtga.


The killing of Dúnlang son of Dúnchú.


Flann Fine, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, died.

U733.10

Do-Chumas of Bolgán, anchorite of Ard Macha, rested.


A cow was seen in Deilginis Cualann with six legs, i.e. with two bodies behind
and a single head in front. An ól-measure of milk was taken from her thrice at
each milking.

# 734 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 733.


Rechtabra grandson of Cathasach, king of Uí Thuirtri, dies.


A lunar eclipse on the eleventh of the Kalends of February.


The bringing on tour of the relics of Peter, Paul and Patrick to fulfil the
law; and the slaying of Coibdenach son of Flann grandson of Congal.


Caintigern, daughter of Cellach of Cualu, dies.


Talorg son of Congus was held captive by his brother, handed over to the Picts,
and drowned by them.


Talorgan son of Drostan is apprehended and manacled near Dún Ollaig.


Dún Leithfinn is destroyed after the wounding of Dúngal, and he fled to Ireland
from the power of Aengus.


An encounter in Mag Itha between Flaithbertach son of Loingsech and Fergal's
son Aed Allán in which the Uí Echdach of Cenél Eógain and others fell.


Taichlech son of Cenn Faelad, king of Luigne, dies.

U734.10

Aed Allán begins to reign.



# 735 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 734.


Oegedchar, bishop of Naendruim, rests.


A battle in the territory of Muirtheimne between the Uí Néill and the Ulaid, in
which Aed Rón, king of Ulaid, and Conchad son of Cuanu, king of Cuib, fell. Aed
son of Fergal was victor.


A battle between Mumu and Laigin, in which many of the Laigin and well nigh
countless Munstermen perished; Cellach son of Faelchar, king of Osraige, fell
therein, but Cathal son of Finnguine, king of Mumu, escaped.


Airechtach grandson of Dúnchad of Muirisc, king of Uí Fhiachrach, and Cathal
son of Muiredach from whom is descended Clann Chathail of Mag Aí, king of
Connacht, die.


The killing of Flann son of Conaing, abbot of Cell Mór Díthrib.


A huge dragon was seen, with great thunder after it, at the end of autumn.


Bede, a learned man of the Saxons, rested.

# 736 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 735.


Aengus son of Fergus, king of the Picts, laid waste the territory of Dál Riata
and seized Dún At and burned Creic and bound in chains two sons of Selbach,
i.e. Donngal and Feradach; and shortly afterwards Bruide son of Aengus son of
Fergus died.


The battle of Cnoc Cairpri in Calathros at Etarlinde between Dál Riata and
Foirtriu, and Talorgan son of Fergus goes in pursuit of Ainfchellach's son who
had taken flight, many nobles falling in this encounter.


Death of Fianamail son of Gerthinne, abbot of Cluain Iraird, and death of
Crunnmael son of Colgu, abbot of Lusca.


Dainél son of Colmán of Int Eidnén(?), abbot of Ard Brecáin, and Colmán, son of
Murchu, abbot of Mag Bile, rested.


The killing of Mael Fothartaig, son of Mael Tuile, by the Laigin.


Duiblitir, a learned man and anchorite of Inis Bó Finne, and Samson, grandson
of Corcrán, fell asleep.


Bodbthach son of Conall of Gabair, king of Cairpre, dies.



# 737 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 736.


Death of Rónán, abbot of Cenn Garad.


Failbe son of Guaire, i.e. successor of Mael Ruba of Apor Crosán, was drowned
in the depth of the sea together with his sailors, twenty-two in number.


Conmall grandson of Lóchéne, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, rests.


An encounter between the descendants of Aed Sláine themselves, in which Conaing
son of Amalgaid defeated Cernach, and Cathal son of Aed fell. The encounter
took place near Lia Ailbi, on the east side.


Muirgius son of Fergus Forcraid was killed.


Bresal son of Conchobor of Ard was slain.


Aengus son of Ailill, king of Ard Ciannachta, dies.


Death of Graiphnech, abbot of Imlech Fia.


A meeting between Aed Allán and Cathal at Tír dá Glas.

U737.10

The law of Patrick was in force in Ireland.


Fiangalach son of Murchad, king of Uí Máil, dies.

# 738 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 737.


Faelán grandson of Bran, king of Laigin, died unexpectedly at an unripe age.


Tóle, bishop of Cluain Iraird, a worthy soldier of the Lord, rests.


Cernach, son of Fogartach, is treacherously killed by his own criminal
adherents, and the calves of the cows and the women of this lower world for
long bewailed him.


The battle of Áth Senaig i.e. the 'battle of groans' on the 14th of the Kalends
of September, the sixth feria, between the Uí Néill and the Laigin was sternly
fought, and the two kings respectively, leaders firm and exalted i.e. Aed Allán
king of Temair and Aed son of Colgu king of Laigin. One of them i.e. Aed Allán,
though wounded, survived triumphant, but the other i.e. Aed son of Colgu was
beheaded by a battle-sword. Then the descendants of Conn enjoyed a tremendous
victory, when in extraordinary fashion they rout, trample, crush, overthrow and
destroy their Laigin adversaries, so much so that almost the entire enemy is
well nigh annihilated, there being a few messengers to bring back the tidings.
And men say that so many fell in this great battle that we find no


comparable slaughter in a single onslaught and fierce conflict throughout all
preceding ages. There fell in the battle, moreover, excellent leaders: Aed son
of Colgu, Bran Bec son of Murchad i.e. two kings of Laigin, Fergus son of
Maenach, Dub dá Crich son of the grandson of Cellach son of Trian, i.e. two
lords of Fothairt, Fiangalach grandson of Mael Aitchen, Conall greatgrandson of
Aithechda, four sons of Flann grandson of Congal, Éladach grandson of Maelodar,
and many others, and for the sake of brevity, they are here omitted.


The killing of Fergus son of Crimthann.


Death of Coscrach son of Naendenach, king of Gailenga.


The battle of Inis in which Fernbeand fell.


Death of Sothchathach grandson of Mael Tuili.


A hosting by Cathal son of Finnguine to Laigin and he took hostages from Faelán
and great treasures.

# 739 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 738.


Fergus Glut, king of Cuib, died from the venomous spittles of wicked people.


Cuanu grandson of Bésán, scribe of Treóit, rests.


The falling asleep of Samthann of Cluain Brónaig, and the falling asleep of the
grandson of Mael Daithnéin, a bishop.


The burning of Domnall's followers in Bodbráith, where Ailill of Brí Leth
perished in the banquet-house.


Death of Ailill son of Tuathal, king of Uí Chremthainn.


Flann son of Cellach son of Crunnmael, bishop of Rechrann, dies.


Talorgan son of Drostan, king of Athfoitle, was drowned, i.e. by Aengus.


Death of Aed son of Garbán.



# 740 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 739.


Domnall went off into clerical life.


The killing of Ailill's grandson, lord of Cenél Fiachach.


An earthquake in Íle on the second of the Ides of April.


Flann grandson of Congal died.


Cú Bretan son of Congus dies; and the death of Cellach son of Secnde, abbot of
Cluain Moccu Nóis.


Dub dá Bairenn, abbot of Fobar, died.


The falling asleep of Mainchéne of Tuaim Gréine.


The falling asleep of the holy Bran of Lann Ela.


Flann of Febail, abbot of Gort Conaich, dies.

# 741 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 740.


Death of Conla of Tethba, and of Amalgaid, king of Conaille.


The killing of Murchad son of Fergal son of Mael Dúin; and Conall son of
Iarlaithe dies.


Death of Flann of Aigle, bishop of Echdruim.


Death of Fuirechtach, superior of Inis Caíl.


Bé Fáil, daughter of Sechnasach, dies.


The battle of Forboros in which fell two sons of Fiannamail, i.e. Indrechtach
and Conall and others.


The killing of Ernán grandson of Eculb.


The battle of Carn Feradaig in which Torcán  Tini Reid fell.


The killing of Ailill Corrach son of Flann, king of Uí Fhailgi.

U741.10

The battle of Druim Cathmail between the Cruithin and the Dál Riata against
Indrechtach. The smiting of the Dál Riata by Aengus son of Forgus.


The body of Petronilla, daughter of St Peter, was translated this year, and
these words, written in Peter's own handwriting, were found in the marble tomb
from which it was taken i.e.  The restingplace of Petronilla, most dearly
beloved daughter.



# 742 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
AD 741.


Death of Airechtach son of Cuanu, superior of Ferna.


The devastation of Cenél Fiachach and of Delbna by the Osraige.


Death of Cathal son of Finnguine, king of Caisel.


Death of Mael Ochtraig, abbot of Cell Fhoibrig.


Death of Cuidgile, scribe and abbot of Lugmad.


Death of Aed Balb i.e. son of Indrechtach son of Muiredach, king of Connacht.


The strangling of Conaing son of Amalgaid, king of Ciannachta.


The killing of Artrí son of Aithechda, king of Uí Chremthainn.


Leprosy in Ireland.

U742.10

The capture of Elffin(?) son of Crop.


The killing of the Cenél Cairpri in Gránairet.

# 743 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 742.


Death of Affrica, abbess of Cell Dara.


The battle of Dam Derg in which Dúngal son of Flann, king of Fir Chúl, and
Fergus son of Oistech fell. Indrechtach, grandson of Conaing, was victor.


Death of Cuiméne grandson of Ciarán, abbot of Rechru.


The battle of Seredmag won by Domnall son of Murchad, i.e. in Cenannas, in
which fell Aed Allán son of Fergal and Cumuscach son of Conchobor, king of Int
Airthir, and Maenach son of Conlaech, king of Uí Chremthainn, and Muiredach son
of Fergus Forcraidh, king of Uí Thuirtre. This was Aed Allan's last stanza:

	If my dear God had spared me
	On the shore of Loch Sailchedáin,
	Were I to commit sin thereafter,
	It would have been like giving valuables to a slave.




A battle between Uí Maine and Uí Fhiachrach of Aidne.


The battle of Lorg between the Uí Ailella and the Gailenga. These four battles
were fought almost in one summer.


The law of Suanach's descendant in force.


Conchenn, daughter of Cellach of Cualu, dies.


The killing of Dub Doithre, king of Uí Briúin.

U743.10

Affiath, abbot of Mag Bile, died.


The taking on tour of the relics of Trian of Cell Deilge; and the bolgach was
rampant.


Domnall son of Murchad begins to reign.

# 744 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 743.


The killing of bishop Laidcnén son of Doinennach, abbot of Saigher.


Domnall again entered clerical life.


The killing of Colmán, bishop of Lesán in Uí Tuirtri.


The battle of Cliú in which Conchobor of the Uí Fhidgente fell.


The battle of Ailén dá Berrach in which Dub da Dos, son of Murgal, fell.


Two grandsons of Cellach of Cualu, Cathal and Ailill, were slain.


The killing of Muirgius son of Ánluan in Tuilén.


Destruction of Corcu Mo-Druad by the Déis.


The law of Ciarán son of the wright, and the law of Brénainn, were enforced
simultaneously by Forgus son of Cellach.

U744.10

Death of Fergus son of Colmán Cutlach, the learned.

# 745 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 744.


A horrible and wonderful sign was seen in the stars at night.


Forannán, abbot of Cluain Iraird, died, and Congus, anchorite of Cluain
Tibrinne.


Cuiméne descendant of Maenach, abbot of Lann Léire, dies.


A battle between the Uí Thuirtri and Int Airthir. Congal son of Éicnech, was
victor, and Cú Chongalt son of the grandson of Cathusach, escaped by flight;
and Bóchaill son of Conchobor, and Ailill grandson of Cathusach fell. The
battle was fought in Inis eter dá Dabul.


Death of Conall of the lovely hair, a scribe.




Death of Cenn Faelad, superior of Druim Cuilinn.


Death of Mac Ind Fherthaigse, abbot of Tech Taille.

# 746 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 745.


The falling asleep of Cormac of Áth Truim.


Dragons were seen in the sky.


Death of Beóchall of Ardachadh.


The falling by one another of Dúngal Feille and Muirchertach son of Cathal. The
Conmaicne fell, and Forgus was defeated but escaped.


A slaughter of the southern Uí Briúin by Fergus.


Death of Mael Anfaid of Cell Achaid Droma Fata.


Kin-slaying at Les Mór.


Death of Dub dá Bairenn grandson of Bécán, abbot of Cluain Eóis.


Death of Aengus son of Tipraite, abbot of Cluain Fhata; and Ciall Tróg, abbot
of Glas Naíden, dies.

U746.10

Death of Sechnusach son of Colgu, king of Uí Cheinnselaig.


Violation of sanctuary at Domnach Pátraic, six captives being hanged.

# 747 A.D.

Kalends of January first feria, fifteenth of the moon. AD 746 alias 747.


Death of Abél, abbot of Imlech Fea.


Death of Muiredach Menn, king of Uí Méith.


Cuanan of Glenn, abbot of Mag Bile, dies.


Aed Muinderg, son of Flaithbertach, king of the North, and Sechnusach son of
Colgu, king of Laigin, died.


Cú Chuimne the learned died.

	Cú Chuimne
	Has read the learned authors right through;
	The other half of the fosterage fee
	He has let go for the sake of nuns.
	All the distraction Cú Chuimne had
	Has left him so that he became sage;
	He has abandoned nuns
	And read the remainder which was before him.

             Cú Chuimne's fostermother recited this.




Rumán son of Colmán, an excellent poet, rested.


Death of Sárán, abbot of Bennchor.


The battle of Carn Ailche won by the Mumu, in which Cairpre son of Cú Dínisc
fell.


Death of Dúnlang son of Dúnchú, king of Cenél Ardgail.

U747.10

Death of Tuathalán, abbot of Cinrigh Móna.


Death of Aed Dub son of Cathal.


Repose of the pious Comán i.e. of In Ros, and repose of Fer dá Crích, abbot of
Dairinis.


Death of Rudgal of the Luigne.


Repose of Iacób ua Farannáin, the greatest preacher of his time.

# 748 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 747.


The drowning of Arascach, abbot of Muicinis Riaguil.


Repose of Cúan Cam, the learned.


Snow of unusual depth so that nearly all the cattle of the whole of Ireland
perished, and the world afterwards was parched by unusual drought.


Death of Indrechtach grandson of Conaing, king of Ciannachta.


The falling asleep of the anchorite Do-Dímóc, abbot of Cluain Iraird, and the
learned Do-Chumae of Cell Dara.


Muirenn daughter of Cellach of Cualu, Írgalach's queen, dies.


The slaying of Congal son of Éicnech, king of Int Airthir, in Ráith Escla.


The law of Suanach's grandson in force in Leth Cuinn.


Flann Foirbthe son of Fogartach, and Cuan, anchorite from Lilcach, die.

# 749 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 748.


The killing of Cathusach son of Ailill, king of the Cruithin, in Ráith
Beithech.


Death of Bresal son of Colgu, abbot of Ferna.


The burning of Cluain Ferta Brénainn.


The burning of Cell Mór of Aedán son of Aengus.




The battle of Ard Ciannachta in which fell Ailill son of Dub dá Crích, king of
Ard Ua Cinn Fhaelad, and also in a counter-attack Domnall son of Cinad, i.e.
the king who had previously been victorious.


Death of Cairpre son of Murchad of Mide; and Béc Buile son of Eochu, and Liber,
abbot of Mag Bile; and a great windstorm.


Drowning of the community of Í.


Death of Conall, abbot of Tuaim Gréine.


Ships with their crews were seen in the air above Cluain Moccu Nóis.

# 750 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 749.


The burning of Fobar, and the burning of Domnach Pátraic.


Death of Suairlech, bishop of Fobar.


Repose of Congus, bishop of Ard Macha.


The battle of Catonic (?) between Picts and Britons, in which Talorgan, son of
Forgus and brother of Aengus, fell.


Death of Cathal of Maenmag, king of Uí Maine.


Eó-inis is abandoned.


Death of Corc's grandson of Cell Dara.


Death of Comarbach son of Cellán, abbot of Cell Mór Einir.


Death of Cú Dínaisc, grandson of Forgus, of the Uí Fhiachrach.

U750.10

The killing of Fiachra son of Ailéne, king of Mugdorna, and of Bresal son of
Aed Rón.


End of the reign of Aengus.


Death of Dub dá Leithe, abbot of Cell Scíre.


Mac Nemnaill, abbot of Biror, dies.


Death of Cú Chothaig son of Maenach.

# 751 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 750.


Death of Flann grandson of Congal, king of Uí Fhailgi.


Death of Fergal or Fergus, son of Fogartach, king of southern Brega.


Burning of half the stored grain of Cluain Iraird in a kiln(?).




Death of Echaid of Cell Tóma.


Death of Céile Do-Laisse from Daiminis.


Death of the abbot of Sláine, Colmán of the Britons, son of Faelán; and Bran
son of Baeth Beithri dies.


Death of Nuadu son of Dub Sléibe, abbot of Cluain Eóis.


Death of Fursa, abbot of Lecain Mide.


Death of Mael Imarchuir, bishop of Echdruim.

# 752 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 751.


Death of Cilléne the bridge-maker(?), anchorite of Í.


Cathal son of Forannán, abbot of Cell Dara, and Cuiméne grandson of Béc, a
devout man of Eig, died.


Death of Dícuill son of Meinide, abbot of Inis Muiredaig, and death of Congus
Caech, scribe and abbot of Liath Mór Mo-Chaemóc.


Death of Fiachna grandson of Maicnia, abbot of Cluain Ferta Brénainn.


Death of Flaithbertach son of Conall Menn, king of Cenél Cairpri.


Indrechtach son of Muiredach Menn, dies.


Death of Foidmenn son of Fallach, king of Conaille Muirtheimne.


Death of Cilléne son of Congal in Í.


Death of Conaing grandson of Dub Dúin, king of Cenél Cairpri of Tethba.

U752.10

Death of Mael Tuile, abbot of Tír Da Glas.


Death of Osbran, anchorite and bishop of Cluain Crema.


Death of Rechtabra grandson of Guaire, abbot of Tuaim Gréine.


Death of the learned Didymus grandson of Lígán, of Cluain.


Destruction of the Brecraige by the Cenél Cairpri, in Tulach Finnin.


Destruction of the Calraige of Lorg by the Uí Briúin.

# 753 A.D.

Kalends of January.
[...]
AD 752.


A dark sun.


The falling asleep of Mac Oiged, abbot of Les Mór.


Repose of Luchraid, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


The law of Colum Cille enforced by Domnall of Mide.




Death of Cellan, abbot of Cluain Ferta Brénainn.


Death of Scannlán of Dún Lethglaisi.


Echaid grandson of Maenach, king of Uí Moccu Uais, dies.


Death of Mo-Baí.


Death of the learned Ferblae, son of Nárgus.

U753.10

The slaughter of the Uí Ailella by the Grecraige.


Death of Scannlaige of Cluain Bairenn.


Death of Fursa of Es Mac nEirc.


A whale was cast ashore in Bairche in the time of Fiachna son of Aed Rón, king
of Ulaid. It had three gold teeth in its head, each containing fifty ounces,
and one of them was placed on the altar of Bennchor this year, that is, in AD
752.

# 754 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 753.


Death of Flann son of Conchobor, king of Mag Aí.


Loingsech son of Flaithbertach, king of Cenél Conaill, dies.


Sleibéne, abbot of Í, came to Ireland.


Repose of Cerpán of Dam Liac.


The killing of Cenn Faelad, grandson of Cuiléne.


Destruction of the Fotharta Fea by the Osraige.


The battle of Ard Naescán between the Uí Briúin and the Cenél Cairpri, in which
many fell.


Death of Abél of Áth Omna.


A battle between the Uí Thuirtri themselves.

# 755 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 754.


Death of Flaithnia son of Tnúthach, king of Uí Méith.


The burning of Cluain Moccu Nóis on the twelfth of the Kalends of April 21
March.


Death of Fiachra of Martharthech.


Felcmaire son of Comgall, the learned Cathal son of Diarmait, Daelgus, abbot of
Cell Scíre, died.


Indrechtach son of Dlúthach, king of Uí Maine, Flaithnia son of Flann grandson
of Congal, king of Uí Fhailgi, Fiangalach son of Anmchad son of Mael Curaich,
abbot of Inis Bó Finne on Loch Rí, Mac Rónchon of Cenél Cairpri, Snéidcheist,
abbot of Naendruim, died.



# 756 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 755.


The burning of Bennchor the Great on St Patrick's Day 17 March.


Forgus son of Cellach, king of Connacht, Ailgal, anchorite of Cluain Chormaic,
Forannán, bishop of Methas Truim, Baethallach son of Colmán, grandson of
Suibne, died.


An army of the Laigin led by Domnall against Niall, and they reached Mag
Muirtheimne.


Shipwreck of the Delbna in Loch Rí, including (?) their leader, i.e. Díumasach,
i.e. with thirty boats, and the crew of only one escaped.


The battle of Móin Mór, in which the Cenél Cairpri were overthrown.

# 757 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 756.


Repose of Fidmuine, i.e. grandson of Suanach, anchorite of Raithen.


Ethelbald, king of the Saxons of Mercia dies.


The burning of Cell Mór Dithraib by the Uí Chremthainn.


Death of Finnchú, abbot of Les Mór.


The battle of Cenn Febrat between the Munstermen themselves:, in which Bodbgal,
superior of Mungairit, fell.


The falling asleep of Siadal of Linn Duachail.


Fergus son of Congal, Tomaltach, king of Ciannachta Glinne Gaimin, the
anchorite Cuidgal, Aildobur, abbot of Muiccert, died.


The killing of Donn son of Cumascach, king of the southern Uí Briúin.


The law of Colum Cille promulgated by Sléibéne.

# 758 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 758.


Niallgus son of Baeth, king of the Déisi of Brega, Muiredach son of Cormac of
Sláine, abbot of Lugmad, Cathal, king of Uí Cheinnselaig, Domnall son of Flann
of the Derg, Eilpín of Glas Naide, Aed son of Cormac, one of two kings of
Ciannachta, Fidbadach of Cell Deilge, died.


Céile Petair from Crích Bresail, abbot of Ard Macha, died.




Marthu daughter of Mac Dubáin, abbess of Cell Dara, died.


The battle of Druim Róbaig between the Uí Fhiachrach and the Uí Briúin, in
which fell Tadc son of Muirdibur, and three grandsons of Cellach, Cathrannach,
Cathmug and Artbran. Ailill grandson of Dúnchad was victor.

# 759 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 758.


Slógadach son of Donngal of Cenél Cairpri, Echaid son of Conall the Stammerer,
abbot of Foibrén, Forduban  Life-loving , Domnall son of Aed of Laigin, the
learned teacher Siadal son of Luath, and the learned Echaid son of Fiachra,
died.


The battle of Emain Macha in which Dúngal grandson of Conaing and Donn Bó were
slain. Fiachna son of Aed Rón was victor.

	Would that exultant Donn Bó had not come
	And forsaken his kingship
	So that he is now in a dwelling of clay
	After the battle of Cúl Chíre.
	As they travel over a path to a mountain
	Or leave the stone church,
	Everyone's eyes that see it his grave
	Have sunk in the head in which they are.
	Happy are you, O cowled little cleric,
	Who are noble when cornered by a serpent,
	Confronting your fierce enemy
	With your back to the citadel.
	Your one side is towards the Erne lakes
	As you shape a course into Mide,
	And your other side
	Is towards the very warlike Glenn Rige.


The killing of Rechtabra son of Dúnchú, king of Mugdorna.


Dub Drumán, abbot of Tuilén, dies.


A rainy summer.


A stream with fishes burst forth from Benn Muilt.


The killing of Ferdamal, son of Cenn Faelad.



# 760 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 759.


A great snowfall on the fourth of the nones 2nd of February.


The skirmish of Foileng, in which fell Dúnchad son of Cathal, and Ceithernach
son of Dothad.


Death of Tairchelltach the learned.


The slaying of Congalach son of Conall, king of Diathrab.


Death of Muiredach grandson of Bran, king of Laigin.


Famine and a great mast-crop.


Death of Conchobor grandson of Tadc of Teimen; and Conait, abbot of Les Mór,
and Gaim Díbáil, abbot of Árainn of Énna, died.


A battle between the communities of Cluain and Biror in Móin Choise Blae.


Condam of Cluain Chuifthin dies.

# 761 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 760.


Death of Fínnechta son of Fogartach grandson of Cernach.


The battle of Áth Duma between the Ulaid and the Uí Echach, in which Ailill son
of Feidlimid fell.


The battle of Belat Gabráin in which Donngal son of Laidcnén, king of Uí
Ceinnselaig, and other kings fell.


Death of Aengus son of Fergus, king of the Picts.

# 762 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 761.


A great snowfall, and a dark moon.


The killing of bishop Eóthigern by a priest in the oratory of Cell Dara.


Repose of Cormac, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


A bright night in autumn.


The battle of Caill Tuidbig in which the Luigne were over-thrown. The Cenél
Cairpri obtained the victory.


The learned Fer Fio son of Faibre, abbot of Comrair Mide, died.


The battle of Sliab Truim.


Robartach son of Cuanu, superior of Othain, dies.



# 763 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 762.


Death of Domnall son of Murchad son of Diarmait Guthbinn son of Airmedach Caech
son of Conall son of Suibne son of Colmán Mór son of Diarmait son of Fergus
Cerrbél, king of Temair, on the twelfth of the Kalends of December.


Death of Bec Laitne, abbot of Cluain Iraird; and Faelchu of Finnglas and
Fidairle grandson of Suanach, abbot of Raithen, died.


Death of Reothaide, abbot of Ferna.


Death of Anfadán, abbot of Linn Duachail.


A dark sun at the third hour of day.


Death of Flann Garad, king of Cenél Meic Erca.


Do-Cheta of Lothra died.


The slaughter of Cuilnech Mór, where the Connachta were overthrown.


A battle between the Uí Fhidgeinte and the Corcu Modruad and the Corcu
Baiscinn.

U763.10

Bruide, king of Foirtriu, dies.


Niall Frosach begins to reign.

# 764 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 763.


A great snowfall which lasted almost three months.


Repose of Rónán, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


Death of Cormac son of Ailill, abbot of Mainistir Buiti.


A great scarcity, and famine.


Death of Dub Deilge the learned.


The battle of Argaman between the community of Cluain Moccu Nóis and the
community of Dermag, in which fell Diarmait Dub son of Domnall, and Diglach son
of Dub Lis, and two hundred men of the community of Dermag. Bresal, son of
Murchad, emerged victor, with the community of Cluain.


An abnormally great drought.


Ailill grandson of Dúnchad, king of Connacht, dies.


Scannlan of Feimen, son of Aedgal, dies.

U764.10

A bloody flux throughout Ireland.


The killing of Bresal son of Murchad.




The battle of Dún Bile won by Donnchad over the Fir Tulach.


Death of Fiachra son of Fothad, abbot of Baislec.


Murchad son of Indrechtach died.


Three showers fell in Crích Muiredaig in Inis Eogain, i.e. a shower of pure
silver, a shower of wheat, and a shower of honey.

	The three showers of Ard Uilinne
	Fell from heaven for love of Niall:
	A shower of silver, a shower of wheat,
	And a shower of honey.
	Fergal's manly son
	Was dubbed for this among warriors:
	Since everyone came to follow them
	Niall of the Showers is his name.
	A hundred hostages from each province
	Was what Niall exacted;
	Daring was the noble pledge he gave(?)
	To exact them three times.

# 765 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 764.


A horrible and wonderful sign was seen in the stars at night.


Death of Flaithbertach son of Loingsech, king of Temair, in religious life.


Repose of Tóla of Ard Brecáin.


The killing of Suibne son of Murchad, with his two sons.


The battle of Carn Fiachach between two sons of Domnall, i.e. Donnchad and
Murchad. Fallomon assisted Donnchad, and Ailgal Murchad. Murchad fell in the
battle, and Ailgal was put to flight.


Folachtach, abbot of Biror, dies.


Loarn, abbot of Cluain Iraird, rested.


Cellbil of Cluain Brónaig died.


A shortage of bread.

# 766 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 765.


The falling asleep of Crimthann, abbot of Cluain Ferta.


The treacherous killing of Follaman son of Cú Chongalt, king of Mide.




The battle of Sruthair between the Uí Briúin and the Conmaicne in which very
many of the Conmaicne fell, and Aed Dub son of Taichlech fell. Dub Innrecht,
son of Cathal, was victor.


A battle between Mide and Brega, in which fell Mael Uma son of Tuathal and
Donngal son of Doiret.


Death of Cernach son of Cathal, and of Cernach son of Flann.


Suibne, abbot of Í, came to Ireland.

# 767 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 766.


Conbrann, abbot of Cell Achaid, dies.


The killing of Murchad son of Flaithbertach, king of Cenél Conaill.


Fidbadach, abbot of Bennchor, rested.


Dub dá. Inber, son of Cormac, abbot of Mainister Buiti, died.


Repose of Sléibéne of Í.


Mac int Shair, abbot of Enach Dub, died.


Glaindibur, abbot of Lathrach Briúin, rests.


Cellach son of Cairpre son of Fogartach was killed by a robber.


Flathgus son of Fiachra son of Cathal was treacherously killed.

U767.10

Patrick's law was enforced.

# 768 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 767.


Dub Innrecht son of Cathal, king of Connacht, dies, i.e. from the bloody flux.


Gormgal son of Ailill dies.


Aedán abbot of Les Mór, and Miannach's grandson, abbot and elder of Cluain
Moccu Nóis, died.


Fer dá Crích son of Suibne, abbot of Ard Macha, rested.


Eithne daughter of Bresal of Brega, the queen of kings of Temair, deserved to
obtain the heavenly kingdom, having done penance.


Coibdenach, abbot of Cell Tóma, rests.


A battle in Foirtriu between Aed and Cinaed.



# 769 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 768.


An overthrow in a battle between the Osraige themselves, in which the sons of
Cellach, son of Faelchar, were put to flight. Tóim Snáma emerged victorious.


The battle of Ferna in which Dub Calgaid son of Laidcnén fell. Ceinselach was
victor.


Repose of Murgal son of Ninnid, abbot of Rechru.


Encorach grandson of Doadán, abbot of Glenn dá Locha, dies.


Cairpre son of Fogartach driven into exile by Donnchad.


An earthquake and famine; and a leprous disease attacked many. Abundance of
oak-mast.


Comán of Enach Daithe, and Conchobor son of Cumascach, king of Aidne, died.

# 770 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 769.


Nargal son of Nat Sluaig dies from the bloody flux.


Tóim Snáma son of Flann king of Osraige was killed.


Artgal, abbot of Clochar Mac nDaiméni, dies.


A battle between the Laigin themselves at Áth Orc, in which Cellach son of
Dúnchad was victor, and Cinaed son of Flann and his brother Cellach and Cathnia
son of Béc and many others fell.


Fiachra of Gránairet, Fergil of Cell Mór Enir, and bishop Fergus son of Cathal
died.


Folachtach of Tech Tua, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, dies.


A battle between the Uí Cheinnselaig, in which Ceinnselach son of Bran fell,
and Eterscél son of Aed son of Colgu was victor.


An encounter between Donnchad son of Domnall and Cellach son of Donnchad, and
Donnchad went with the army of the Uí Néill against the Laigin and the Laigin
eluded him, and went to Scé Nechtain. The Uí Néill remained seven days in Ráith
Ailinne, and burned all the confines of the Laigin with fire.


The overthrow of the men of southern Brega at Bolg Bóinne, in which fell
Flaithbertach son of Flann son of Ragallach, and Uarchride son of Baeth and
Snédgus son of Ainfthech and Cernach son of Flann Foirbthe.



U770.10

The overthrow of the Uí Téig by the Ciannacht at Áth Cliath. There was a great
slaughter of the Laigin. A number of the Ciannacht were drowned in the full
tide as they returned.


Gormán daughter of Flann son of Aed died.


Bishop Crunnmael, abbot of Cell Mór Einir, rested.


Death of Connmach son of Brénainn, abbot of Cluain Dochra.


Mael Ditin son of Dub Innrecht was killed.


Death of Béc's grandson, abbot of Fobar.

# 771 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 770.


Death of Flann grandson of Do-Chua, abbot of Inis Caín Dega.


Aedgein of Fobar died.


Béc son of Conla, king of Tethba, dies.


Cairpre son of Fogartach, king of Brega, dies.


Forbasach grandson of Cernach, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, dies.


Aengus son of Fogartach, king of Cenél Loegaire, died suddenly.


Cathal son of Conall Menn, king of Cairpre Mór, and Dúngalach son of Taichlech,
chief of Luigne, died.


Coblaith daughter of Cathal, abbess of Cluain Cuifthin, died.


Allcellach of Telach Ualann, and Aichlech grandson of Cenn Fiaich, died.

U771.10

A hosting by Donnchad into In Fochla.

# 772 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 771.


Death of Airlid of Cluain Iraird.


The killing of Conchobor, grandson of Mael Dúin.


The hosting of Donnchad to Cnoc Báine.


The assembly of the 'hand-clapping' at which occurred lightning and thunder
like the day of judgment. The 'hand-clapping' on St Michael's Day 29 Sept.
which called fire from heaven.


Death of Suibne, abbot of Í.


The Irish fasted two periods of three days, with a single meal between them,
through fear of the fire.


Repose of Mael Aichthen, abbot of Cluain Eidnig.




The law of Comán and Aedán enforced a second time on the three Connachta.


Aed Aithgein, king of Uí Maine, and Art son of Flaithnia, king of Aidne, were
killed.

U772.10

Lergus, son of Dub Comair, dies.

# 773 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 772.


Maenach son of Colmán, abbot of Sláine and Cell Fhoibrich, dies from the bloody
flux.


Dainél grandson of Foiléne, scribe of Letuba, rested.


Dorm Cothaid, king of Connacht, dies.


Unaccustomed drought and heat of the sun so that nearly all bread-grain failed.
Abundance of oak-mast afterwards.


Martin of Inis Eidnech, Aedán, bishop of Mag Eó, Ceithernach grandson of
Erumain, abbot of Cluain Ferta Brénainn, died.


Lerthan, abbess of Cell Dara, died.


A dark moon on the second of the nones 4th of December.


Aed son of Cairpre, superior of Rechru, dies.

# 774 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 773.


Death of Albran son of Foidmed, abbot of Treóit Mór, on the sixth feria between
the two Easters 8 April.


Ultán grandson of Bir Derg, abbot of Othain Mór, Éireannach son of Éichen,
abbot of Leithglenn, Forannán, scribe and bishop of Treóit, died.


Dúnlaith, daughter of Fogartach, died.


Tomaltach son of Murgal, king of Cruachain Aí, Bodbchad son of Échtgus, king of
Cenél Meic Erca, die.


Suairlech grandson of Cú Chiaráin, abbot of Les Mór, and Imráithech of Glenn
Clóitige, an anchorite, die.


Flathrua son of Fiachra, king of the Cruithin, dies.


Disturbance of the fair by Donnchad.


Eógan son of Colmán died of the bloody flux, and many others died of that same
illness.



# 775 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 774.


Death of Cinadu, king of the Picts; and Donngal son of Nuadu, abbot of Lugmad,
and Fianchú, abbot of Lugmad, and Conall of Mag Luinge, and Suairlech, abbot of
Linn, died.


The burning of Ard Macha.


The burning of Cell Dara.


The burning of Glenn dá Locha.


An encounter between the Munstermen and the Uí Néill, and Donnchad did great
devastation in the territory of the Munstermen, and many of the Munstermen
fell.


A skirmish in Cluain Iraird between Donnchad and the community of Cluain
Iraird.


Repose of Ciarán the devout i.e. of Belach Dúin.


The battle of Achad Liac between the Uí Briúin and the Uí Maine, in which the
Uí Maine were overthrown.


A slaughter of the Uí Macc mBrocc in the time of Colgu son of Cellach.

U775.10

The burning of Inis Baíthéni.

# 776 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 775.


Repose of Colmán the Fair, an anchorite.


Death of Gaedel of Cluain Iraird.


Death of Forbasach, abbot of Ráith Aeda.


Death of Collbrann, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


The taking on tour of the relics of St Erc of Sláine and of the relics of
Finnian of Cluain Iraird.


Death of Mael Manach, abbot of Cenn Garad.


A battle between the Dál nAraide themselves on Sliab Mis, in which Nial son of
Cú Allaid fell. The battle of Drong again in the same year between the Dál
nAraidi, in which Cinaed of Carraic son of Cathasach, and Dúngal grandson of
Fergus Forcraidh fell. Tomaltach son of Indrechtach, and Echaid son of Fiachna,
were victors.


Cellach son of Dúnchad, king of Laigin, dies.


The battle of Áth Duma between Int Airthir and Uí Echach Coba, in which Gormgal
son of Conall Cru, king of Cuib, fell.



U776.10

Eógan son of Rónchenn, abbot of Les Mór, and Mael Ruba, grandson of Maenach,
died.


A destructive battle between the Uí Néill and Mumu, in which the community of
Dermag, Tobaeth's sons i.e. Duinechaid and Cathrannach, and some of Domnall's
sons were engaged; and many from Munster fell, and the victors were the Uí
Néill.


Death-struggle of the Cú's.

# 777 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 776.


The killing of Cumascach's son(s) at Odra; one survived, the other dies.


A battle-skirmish occurs between two grandsons of Cernach i.e. Niall and
Cumuscach, in which Échtgus son of Baeth and many others fell in the green of
Caladruim.


A hosting of the Laigin by Donnchad against Brega.


The whole winter in summer, i.e. heavy rain and windstorm.


Death of Flaithrui son of Domnall, king of Connacht.


Disturbance of the assembly by Donnchad against the Ciannacht. Warfare between
Donnchad and Congalach.


A slaughter of the Calraige by the Uí Fhiachrach.


Bán Boidbgni the learned died.


The bloody flux; also many other diseases almost a mortality. A great murrain
of cows.

# 778 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 777.


The same warfare between Donnchad and Congalach i.e son of Conaing, and at its
conclusion the battle of Forchalad in which fell Congalach son of Conaing and
Cuanu son of Éicnid and Diarmait son of Clothgna and Dúnchad son of Ailéne and
Flaithnia son of Mael Dúin and many others. Donnchad was victor.

	From the battle of Forchalad which was fought
	On a gloomy sad Sunday
	Many a fond mother was sorrowful
	The Monday on the morrow.


The burning of Cluain Moccu Nóis on the sixth of the Ides 10th of July.


The bloody flux; the great murrain of cows.




The law of Colum Cille promulgated by Donnchad and Bresal.


Death of Etarscéle son of Aed son of Colgéne, king of Uí Ceinnselaig.


The falling asleep of Ainfchellach, abbot of Condaire and Lann Ela.


Niall Frosach son of Fergal in Í Choluim Chille, and Niall son of Conall Grant,
king of southern Brega, and Tuathal son of Cremthann, king of Cuala, and
Flannabra, king of Uí Máil, and Aed Finn son of Echaid, king of Dál Riata all
died.


Síth Maith, abbess of Cluain Bairenn, died.


Fínán, abbot of Cluain Eóis, and the learned Constans of Loch nÉirne, rested.

U778.10

A slaughter of the Uí Maine in Mag Dairben, where Artgal was victor.


Eithne, daughter of Cinad, dies.

# 779 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 778.


Death of Fulartach, bishop of Cluain Iraird.


Aengus son of Ailéne, king of Mugdorna, Conall grandson of Oiséne, abbot of
Letuba, Ainmere, abbot of Ráith Nua, Conna son of Conán, Maenán son of Cormac,
abbot of Fursa's monastery in France, and Flaithrua, king of Connacht, died.


The murrain of cows did not cease, and there was a mortality of men from want.


The burning of Cell Dara on the third of the Ides 11th of June.


The burning of Cluain Mór Maedóc.


The burning of Cell Deilge.


The smallpox throughout Ireland.


A great windstorm at the end of autumn.


Muiredach son of Aengus, king of Ard Ciannachta, was killed.

U779.10

A hosting by Donnchad into In Fochla, and he took hostages from Domnall son of
Aed Muinderg, king of the North.


A battle between the Munstermen themselves, in which Fergal son of Éladach,
king of Desmumu, fell. Breislén of Béirre was victor.


Forbasach son of Mael Tóla, abbot of Ros Cam, died.



# 780 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 779.


The burning of Ail Cluaithe on the Kalends 1st of January.


The burning of Cluain Bairenn and the burning of Balna.


Death of Murchad, son of Dub da Tuath.


Colgu son of Cellach defeated the Airthir in a battle in which many of the
lower orders fell.


Eilpín, king of the Saxons, dies.


Mac Leinne, abbot of Inis Bairenn, died.


The flight of Ruaidrí from Óchtar Ocha, and of Cairpre son of Laidcnén, with
two septs of the Laigin. Donnchad pursued them with his adherents, and laid
waste and burned their territory and churches.

	Great hosts (the game continues)
	Pour forth for themselves streams of gore;
	A company parts with Life
	In the overthrow of Óchtar Ocha.


A great snowfall in April.


Fergus of Mag Duma dies.

U780.10

Forblaith daughter of Connla, abbess of Cluain Brónaig, dies.


Augustin of Bennchor, and Sédrach son of Sobarthan, and Nádarchú the learned,
died.


A congress of the synods of Uí Néill and Laigin, in the town of Temair, at
which were present many anchorites and scribes, led by Dubliter.


Maicnia son of Cellach, abbot of Dún Lethglaisi, rested.


Third enforcement of the law of Comán and Aedán begins.

# 781 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 780.


Dúngalach, son of Congal, dies.


A great disturbance in Ard Macha on Quinquagesima day, in which Condálach son
of Ailill fell.


Senchán, abbot of Imlech Ibair, and Órach, abbot of Les Mór; the abbot of Inis
Daimle, and Saergal grandson of Edairngne, abbot of Cluain Ferta Mo-Lua, and
Dub Innrecht son of Fergus, abbot of Ferna, and Ailngnad, bishop of Ard
Brecáin, and Maenach grandson of Maenach, abbot of Lann Léire, and Fechtach,
abbot of Fobar, and


Colgu son of Cellach, king of Uí Chremthainn, and Ailbran son of Lugaid, abbot
of Cluain Dolcáin, Nuadu grandson of Bolcán, abbot of Tuaim da Ólann, Dúngal
son of Flaithnia, king of Uí Máil, the learned Saergal grandson of Cathal, and
Fergus son of Eochu, king of Dál Riata all died.


The battle of the Rig, gained by the men of Brega over the Laigin on All
Hallows Day, in which Cú Chongalt, king of Ráith Inbir, fell. Diarmait son of
Conaing, and Conaing son of Dungal two grandsons of Conaing and Mael Dúin son
of Fergus, and Fogartach son of Cumuscach two grandsons of Cernach were victors
in the battle of the Rige.

	The Laigin went for Samhain
	To the house of a good man whom they did not love,
	Scarcity of drink did not affect them,
	They remained on the bank of the Rig.


Éicnech son of Éistenach, steward of Dam Liac, and Cú Dínaisc ua Ciarraige,
die.

# 782 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 781.


Uarchride, grandson of Mael Toile, and Cormac son of Bresal, abbot of Ard
Brecáin and other monastic cities, and Dub Tholarg, king of the Picts on this
side of Monoth, and Muiredach son of Uargal, steward of Í, and Bécán of
Lifechair, and Scannal grandson of Tadc, abbot of Achad Bó on the feast of
Comgall, in his 43rd year of office and Banbán, abbot of Claenad, and Aedán
abbot of Ros Comáin, and Ultán, steward of Bennchor, and Ferdomnach of Tuaim dá
Gualann all died.


Artgal son of Cathal, king of Connacht, took the pilgrim's staff, and made his
pilgrimage in the following year to the island of Í.


The battle of Cuirrech in the vicinity of Cell Dara on the sixth of the Kalends
of September 27 Aug., the third feria, between Ruaidrí son of Faelán, and Bran
son of Muiredach. Therein fell Mugrón son of Flann, king of Uí Fhailgi, and Dub
dá Crích son of Laidcnén in opposing him. Ruaidrí was victor; Bran was led away
captive.



# 783 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 782.


The killing of Domnall son of Flaithnia, king of Uí Fhailgi, in Cluain Conaire
of Maeldub in captivity (?)


Aengus son of Crunnmael, abbot of Dam Liac, and Ailill grandson of Tipraite,
and Suairlech a celebrated anchorite of Les Mór, and the learned Bathallach of
Senchua, and Domnall son of Ceithernach, king of Uí Garrchon (in clerical
life), and the learned Rechtlaiten of Fobar, and the learned Aaron, and the
learned Faelgus son of Tnúthgal of Cluain Iraird, and Fergus, bishop of Dam
Liac, and Béc son of Cumuscach all died.


The burning of Ard Macha and Mag Eó na Saxan.


Terrible lightning (?) throughout Saturday night and thunder on the fourth of
the nones 2nd of August, and a very violent windstorm destroyed the monastery
of Cluain Brónaig.


The battle of Duma Achad between the Dál nAraide themselves, in which Fócarta,
grandson of Cú Allaid, fell.


A battle in Ferna Mór between the abbot and the steward, i.e. between Cathal
and Fiannachtach.


Maenach grandson of Maenach, king of Uí Meic Uais, and Flaithnia's son, abbot
of Cluain Ferta, die.


The scamach was prevalent.


Promulgation of Patrick's law in Cruachain by Dub da Leithi and Tipraite son of
Tadc.

# 784 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 783.


Rechtnia, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, dies.


Mael Dúin son of Aengus, king of Cenél Laegaire, and Innrechtach son of
Dúnchad, and Ciarán, abbot of Ráith Maige Aenaig and Tech Mo-Fhinnu, and
Aedgal, king of Umall, and Cernach son of Suibne, steward of Ard Macha, and
Coisenmech grandson of Predéne, king of Uí Echach Ulad, and Mael Caech, son of
Cuscraid Menn, and Conall son of Crunnmael, abbot of Lusca, and Cú Gamna son of
Naenennach, king of Cenél Cairpri all died.


The burning of Áth Truim.


A battle won by Donnchad son of Aed Muinderg over the Cenél Bogaine.




Dúnchad son of Dub dá Tuath, king of Uí Maine, took the pilgrim's staff.


The learned bishop Flann, abbot of Inis Caín Dega, was killed by poison.


The battle of Carn Conaill in Aidne, in which Tipraite was victorious and the
Uí Fhiachrach defeated.


A royal meeting between Donnchad son of Domnall and Fiachna son of Aed Rón at
Inis na Ríg in eastern Brega.

	What is the meaning
	Of the meeting at Inis na Ríg?
	Donnchad cannot go on the sea
	And Fiachna cannot come ashore.


The coming of the relics of Erc's son to the city of Tailtiu.

# 785 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 784.


Dúnchad grandson of Daiméne, king of Uí Maini, Mael Ochtraig son of Conall,
abbot of Cell Chuilinn and Cell Manach, scribe, and Mael Dúin son of Fergus,
king of Loch Gabor, and the learned Faelgus grandson of Roichlech, and the
learned Mugthigern son of Cellach, abbot of Inis Celtra, and the learned Ioseph
grandson of Foíléne, abbot of Biror, and Ruaidrí son of Faelán, king of all the
Laigin, and Conchobor son of Colcu all died.


The taking on tour of the relics of Ultán.


The battle of the Muad, in which Tipraite was victor.


Echaid son of Fócartach, abbot of Fochlaid and Inis Clothrann, dies.


Ellbríg, abbess of Cluain Brónaig, died.

# 786 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 785.


Mael Dúin son of Aed Bennán, king of Irluachair, Scannlán son of Flann, king of
Uí Fhidgente, and Tipraite son of Ferchar, abbot of Cluain Ferta Brénainn, and
Cellach son of Maenach, and Tipraite son of Tadc, king of Connacht,
Snéidriagail, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis all died.


Cellach son of Cormac, king of Ard Ciannachta, dies.


A great windstorm in January. An inundation in Dairinis. A horrible vision in
Cluain Moccu Nóis, and great penance done throughout Ireland.




A battle between the Osraige themselves, in which Faelán son of Forbasach fell.


Febordaith, abbot of Tuilén, was killed, and he or it was avenged.


The battle of Lia Finn i.e. Tuilén, Donnchad was victor, between Donnchad and
the descendants of Aed Sláine, in which fell Fiachra son of Cathal and
Fócartach son of Cumuscach, king of Loch Gabor, and two grandsons of Conaing,
i.e. Conaing and Diarmait.


The battle of Cenu between the Uí Echach and the Conaille, in which fell
Cathrua, king of Mugdorna, and Rímid, son of Cernach.


Death of Forbasach son of Sechnusach, king of Cenél Bógaine.


A pestilence called scamach.

# 787 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 786.


Colgu son of Crunnmael, abbot of Lusca, Clemens son of Coirbéne, the learned
Lergus grandson of Fidchain of Cell Maighnenn, Robartach son of Maenach,
steward of Sláine and abbot of Cell Fhoibrig, Muiredach son of Cathal, abbot of
Cell Dara, Lomthuili, bishop of Cell Dara, Snéidbran, bishop of Cell Dara,
Aldchú anchorite of Ráith Aenbó all died.


Conall son of Fidgal, king of Uí Maini, died.


A battle between Cenél Conaill and Cenél Eógain, in which Mael Dúin son of Aed
Allán was victor, and Domnall son of Aed Muinderg was put to flight.


The battle of Gola in which the Uí Briúin were defeated. Cathmug son of Donn
Chothaig, and Dub Díbeirg, son of Cathal, fell by one another.


Dub dá Bairenn, abbot of Cluain Iraird, visited the parochia in the territory
of Mumu.


A slaughter of the Uí Briúin of Umall by the Uí Fhiachrach of Muirisc, in which
all the nobles, including king Flaithgal son of Flannabra, fell.


Rechtabra son of Dub Comair, abbot of Echdruim, died.

# 788 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 787.


Death of Mael Dúin son of Aed Allán, king of In Fochla.


Death of Cernach son of Cathal.




Death of Échtgal son of Baeth, abbot of Muicert.


The moon became red like blood on the twelfth of the Kalends of March.


Mac Óc, abbot of Saiger, dies.


Colum son of Faelgus, bishop of Lothra, dies.


Death of Guaire son of Dúngalach, king of Uí Briúin Chualann.


Bishop Dub dá Tuath, abbot of Ráith Aeda, rested.


Ciarán's law in force in Connacht.

U788.10

The burning of Daire Calgaid.

# 789 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 788.


Death of Murgal, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


Fiachna son of Aed Rón, king of Ulaid, dies.


Fedach, son of Cormac, abbot of Lugmad and Sláine and Dam Liac, and Slóigedach
king of Conaille, died.


Gormgal son of Éladach, king of Cnodba, died in clerical life.


Fer Fugaill, bishop of Cluain Dolcáin, died.


The burning of Cluain Iraird on Easter night 18-19 April.


A great snowfall on the third of the Kalends of May 29 April.


A quarrel in Ard Macha, in which a man was killed in front of the stone
oratory.


A battle between the Ulaid themselves in which Tomaltach son of Cathal fell.
Echaid was victor.

U789.10

The slaughter at Cluain Ferta Mongáin by Aengus son of Mugrón, in which Aed son
of Tomaltach fell, and the oratory was burned.


A battle between the Picts, in which Conall son of Tadc was defeated and
escaped; and Constantín was victor.


The battle of Clóitech between Cenél Eógain and Cenél Conaill in which Cenél
Conaill were overthrown, and Domnall escaped i.e. the battle of Clóitech won by
Aed Oirdnide.


The burning of Inis Caín Dega.


Fergil, abbot of Achad Bó, dies.


A battle between the Laigin Desgabair in which Aengus son of Murchad fell.




A battle i.e. of Druim Góise between the Connachta, in which Fogartach son of
Cathal was defeated and escaped. A slaughter of the Luigne by the Uí Ailella in
Achad Abla.


Dishonouring of the staff of Jesus and relics of Patrick by Donnchad son of
Domnall at Ráith Airthir at an assembly.

# 790 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 789.


Death of Nóe, abbot of Cenn Garad.


Cormac, son of Fergal; Dúngal son of Lóegaire, abbot of Dún Lethglaise; Mael
Combair, abbot of Glenn dá Locha; Mael Tuili son of Aengus; Siadal, abbot of
Duiblinn; Cinaed son of Anmchad, king of Uí Liatháin; Tomaltach son of
Innrechtach, king of Dál nAraidi all died.


The battle of Áth Rois won by the Uí Ailella over the Luigni, in which Dub da
Tuath son of Ailill or of Flaithgusa, chief of the Three Septs, fell.


The burning of Echdruim Mac nAeda.


The taking on tour of the relics of Caemgein and of Mo-Chua moccu Lugedon.


A great slaughter of the Ulaid by the Dál nAraidi.


The battle of Conall and Custantín is entered here in other books.

# 791 A.D.

Kalends of January. AD 790.


Cernach son of Muiredach; Frecmarc, bishop of Lusca; Cú Dínaisc son of Cú
Fásaig, abbot of Ard Macha; Donngal son of Buachaill, king of Int Airthir;
Artgal son of Cathal, king of Connacht, in Í; Saerberg, abbot of Cluain Moccu
Nóis; Caínchomrac, bishop of Finnglas; Sirne, abbot of Bennchor; Muiredach son
of Aengus, abbot of Lusca all died.


Bachaill son of Tuathal dies.


Amalgaid, king of Uí Maini, dies.


The battle of Ard Ablae in which Diarmait son of Béc, king of Tethba, fell, and
Fergus son of Ailgal was victor.


A battle overthrow was inflicted in Tailtiu Ducairn Meic Cairthinn (?) by
Donnchad on Aed Ingor, in which Cathall son of Echaid, king of Uí Chremthainn,
and Mael Fothartaig son of Artri, and Domnall son of Colgu fell.


The anchorite Dínertach son of Mogadach rested.



# 792 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 791.


Mael Ruain of Tamlachta, Aedán of Rathan, Aedán grandson of Cú Chumbu bishops
and soldiers of Christ fell asleep in peace; and Saermug of Enach Dub dies.


The battle of Sruithe (?) Cluana Argaid in which Cinaed son of Artgal fell and
Muirgius son of Tomaltach was victor; and the beginning of his reign.


The battle of Ard Maicc Rimi in which the Uí Ailella were overthrown and
Conchobor and Airechtach grandsons of Cathal fell. Cathmug son of
Flaithbertach, king of Cairpre, and Cormac son of Dub dá Crích, king of
Breifne, also fell.


Bresal son of Flaithrí, king of Dál nAraidi, Mael Bresail son of Aed son of
Críchán, king of Uí Fhiachrach, Dorm Corci, king of Dál Riata, Cathmug, king of
Calraige, Téróc, superior of Corcach Mór, died.

# 793 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 792.


Dub dá Leithei son of Sínach, abbot of Ard Macha, Crunnmael of Druim Inasclann,
abbot of Cluain Iraird, Cairpre son of Laidcnén, king of Laigin Desgabair,
Doimthech, superior of Treóit Mór, Cinaed son of Cumascach, abbot of Dermag,
Flaithgel son of Taichlech, abbot of Druim Rátha, died.


The law of Comán promulgated by Aildobur and Muirgius in the three Connachta.


The law of Ailbe promulgated in Mumu, and Artri son of Cathal ordained king of
Mumu.


The profanation of Faendelach by Gormgal son of Dindanach; and Ard Macha was
entered and invaded, and people were slain in it by the Uí Chremthainn.
Faendelach was received again in Ard Macha.


The taking on tour of the relics of Tóla.

# 794 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 793.


Airechtach grandson of Faelán alias grandson of Fledach, abbot of Ard Macha,
and Affiath, bishop of Ard Macha, fell asleep peacefully on the same night.


Tomás, abbot of Bennchor, and Ioseph grandson of Cerna abbot of Cluain Moccu
Nóis, died.




Cathnia grandson of Guaire, abbot of Tuaim Gréine, and Lerben, female superior
of Cluain Bairenn, rested.


The killing of Artrí, son of Faelán.


The taking on tour of the relics of Trian.


A hosting by Donnchad to help the Laigin against the Munstermen.


Devastation of all the islands of Britain by heathens.


The invasion of Mugdorna Maigen by Aed son of Niall.

# 795 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 794.


Bran Airdchenn, king of Laigen, was slain with his queen, Eithne daughter of
Domnall of Mide. Fínnechta Cetharderc, son of Cellach, killed them in Cell
Chúile Duma on the sixth night after the Kalends 6th of May i.e. on the fourth
feria Wednesday.


The slaying of Conn son of Donnchad in Crích Ua nOlcán by Flann son of
Congalach.


The burning of Rechru by the heathens, and Scí was overwhelmed and laid waste.


Faendelach son of Maenach, abbot of Ard Macha, died suddenly.


Murchad son of Feradach, Tipraite son of Ferchar from Cluain Ferta Brénainn,
Guaire grandson of Tipraite, abbot of Cluain Fhata, died.

# 796 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 795.


Dubliter of Finnglas, and Colgu grandson of Duinechaid, and Óchobur son of
Flann son of Erc, king of Mumu scribes, bishops and anchorites fell asleep.


Offa, a good king of the Angles, dies.


The steward of Ard Macha, Echu son of Cernach, died prematurely.


Senchán, abbot of Cell Achaid Droma Fata and Biror, and Suibne, abbot of Áth
Truim, and Maenach son of Aengus, vice-abbot of Lusca, all died.


Forgus son, king of Uí Briúin, and Duinechaid grandson of Daire, chief of
Ciarraige, died.


The battle of Áth Féne, in which Muirgius escaped.


Clothchú, bishop and anchorite of Cluain Iraird, rested in peace.



# 797 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 796.


Death of Donnchad son of Domhall, king of Temair, and of his brother,
Innrechtach son of Domnall.

	(Since the beginning of the fair rational world
	Five thousand proud year
	(A firm distinct trail here)
	Until the death of Domnall's good son was heard of.)


Cumuscach son of Fogartach, king of the south of Brega (in clerical life),
Rothechtach of Craeb, and Muiredach son of Flann Garad, king of Cenél Meic
Erca, and Crunnmael son of Fer dá Crích, and Cú Raí son of Aengus, king of
Cenél Loegaire, and Ailmedair, steward of Cluain Moccu Nóis, all died.


The battle of Druim Ríg in which fell two sons of Domnall, i.e. Fínnechta and
his brother Diarmait Odar, and Fínnechta son of Follaman, and many others who
are not listed. Aed son of Niall son of Fergal was victor.

	(Though Aed (i.e. Allán) fell
	By Domnall (i.e. son of Murchad)
 	A fierce triumph righteous Aed the Fair
	Obtained requital in the battle of Druim Ríg.)


Condal daughter of Murchad, abbess of the elders' house at Cell Dara, fell
asleep.


The devastation of Mide by Aed son of Niall, and the beginning of his reign.

# 798 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 797.


Eudus, grandson of Dícuill, abbot of Cell Dara, dies.


The burning of Inis Pátraic by the heathens, and they took the cattle-tribute
of the territories, and broke the shrine of Do-Chonna, and also made great
incursions both in Ireland and in Alba.


Forinnán of Imlech Fia, and Connmach son of Muirmid grandson of Guaire of
Aidne, scribe of Cluain Moccu Nóis, died.



# 799 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 798.


The battle of Dún Ganiba between the Connachta themselves in which Coscrach son
of Donn, and Gaiscedach, and many others fell; and Muirgius son of Tomaltach
was victor.


The battle of Finnabair in Tethba, where many kings were slain, i.e. Fergus son
of Ailgal, Coscarach son of Ceithernach, and kings of Cenél Cairpri, i.e. Dub
Innrecht son of Artgal and Murchad son of Connmach. Murchad son of Domnall was
victor.


The killing of Blathmac son of Guaire, abbot of Cluain Fhata Baetáin, by Mael
Ruanaid and Follaman son of Donnchad.


A great snowfall in which many men and cattle perished.


Domnall son of Donnchad was treacherously killed by his kinsmen.


Feradach son of Ségéne, abbot of Rechru, died.


Anaile, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, Ceithernach, abbot of Glenn dá Locha,
Siadal grandson of Comán, abbot of Cell Achaid, Fiannachtach of Ferna, Suibne
of Cell Deilge, and Breislén of Béirre, ended their lives.


The  hand-clapping on St. Michael's Day, called the  fire from heaven .


Patrick's law promulgated in Connacht by Gormgal son of Dindathach.

U799.10

Ailill son of Innrechtach, king of Uí Maini of Connacht, dies.


Dúnfhlaith, daughter of Flaithbertach son of Loingsech, fell asleep.

# 800 A.D.

Kalends of January. AD 799.


Airmedach, abbot of Bennchor, Connla son of Artgal, Aildobur, abbot of Ros
Comáin, Mimtenacha, abbot of Glenn dá Locha, died.


Loingsech son of Fiachna, abbot of Dún Lethglaise, Connmach son of Donat, abbot
of Corcach Mór, died.


Feirgil grandson of Tadc, scribe of Lusca, fell asleep.


Ailill son of Fergus, king of the south of Brega, was thrown from his horse
around the Feast of MacCuilinn of Lusca, and died immediately.




A skirmish between Cenél Laegaire and Cenél Ardgail in which Fiangalach son of
Dúnlang fell. Conall son of Niall and Congalach son of Aengus were victors. It
was fought on account of the killing of his brother, i.e., Faílbe.


The placing of the relics of Conlaed in a shrine of gold and silver.


An overthrow in a battle in Mag Lingsen between the Airthir themselves, in
which Mael Ochtraig, abbot of Daire Eidnech, and Conmal son of Cernach fell.

# 801 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 800.


The placing of the relics of Rónán son of Berach in a gold and silver casket.


Feidlimid grandson of Lugaid, abbot of Cluain Dolcáin, dies.


A battle between the Ulaid and the Uí Echach Cobha, in which Echu son of
