Ailill, king of Cuib, fell, and on the opposing side in battle Cairell son of
Cathal fell; and his army was victorious.


Bresal son of Ségéne, abbot of Í, fell asleep in his 31st year as superior.


Ruamnus, abbot of Domnach Sechnaill, dies.


Be Fáil daughter of Cathal, Donnchad's queen, died.


Bresal son of Gormgal of Cenél Loegaire was treacherously killed by his
kinsmen.


Cathrannach son of Cathal of Maenmag, and the anchorite Ninnid, rest.


A rainy summer.

# 802 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 801.


Muiredach son of Domnall, king of Mide, dies.


A hosting by Aed against Mide, and he divided Mide between two sons of
Donnchad, i.e. Conchobor and Ailill.


Ailill son of Cormac, abbot of Sláine, a learned man and an excellent judge,
died.


Fergal son of Anmchad, king of Osraige, dies.


Mac Oige of Aporcrosan, abbot of Bennchor, Muiredach son of Óchobor, abbot of
Cluain Ferta Brénainn, Coscrach grandson of Fraech, abbot of Lugmad, Clemens of
Tír dá Glas all ended their lives happily and in peace.


Artrí son of Ailill, king of Mugdorna Maigen, dies.




Euginis daughter of Donnchad, queen of the king of Temair, dies.


Destruction of the fortress of Loch Riach by Muirgius.


Í Coluim Chille was burned by the heathens.

# 803 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 802.


Repose of Flann son of Nárgal, who lived through painful illness lasting
sixteen years.


Airfhinnán, abbot of Tamlachta Maíl Ruain, rested in peace.


Dúnchad son of Congal, king of Loch Cal, was killed by his kinsmen.


Artgal son of Cathusach, king of Inis Cuilenrigi, of the Cenél Eógain, was
killed.


The battle of Ruba Conaill between two sons of Donnchad, in which Ailill fell
and Conchobur was victor.


Aengus son of Mugrón, king of Uí Fhailgi, was treacherously killed by the
followers of Fínnechta son of Cellach, at their king's instigation.


A skirmish between the Sogain and the sept of Maenmag, in which many were
slain.

# 804 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 803.


Domnall son of Aed Muinderg, king of the North, dies.


Fiachra son of Tuathal, king of Uí Téig, dies.


Repose of Caratbran, abbot of Biror.


Cormac son of Conall, steward of Lusca, dies.


Devastation of Laigin twice in one month by the son of Niall.


Faelán son of Cellach, superior of Cell Dara, and Cernach son of Dúnchad, king
of Mugdorna, died.


A meeting of the synods of the Uí Néill in Dún Cuair, presided over by
Connmach, abbot of Ard Macha.


(This year, moreover, the clerics of Ireland were freed by Aed Oirdnide, at the
behest of Fothad of the Canon, from the obligation of attendance on expeditions
and hostings.)




A battle between the Uí Chremthainn themselves, in which Echu son of Cathal and
Domnall son of Echaid and Dub Rois and many others fell; and Donn Clochair, son
of Artrí, was victor.

U804.10

A hosting by Aed oirdnide to Laigin, and Fínnechta, king of Laigin, submitted
to him.


Violent thunder, accompanied by wind and fire, on the night before St.
Patrick's Day, which destroyed many persons, i.e. one thousand and ten in Corcu
Baiscinn; and the sea divided the island of Fita into three parts, and covered
the land of Fita with sand, that is as much land as would support 12 cows.


Cenannas was given without battle to the melodious Colum Cille this year.

# 805 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
.AD 804.


Muiredach son of Aimirgein, abbot of Leithglenn, Dub Dúin son of Irgus, die.


Cuana, abbot of Mainister Buite, died.


Maenach son of Colgu, abbot of Lusca, a good lector, ended his life unhappily
and mournfully.


Fine, abbess of Cell Dara, died.


Dub dá Bairenn, grandson of Dubán, superior of Cluain Iraird, was added to his
fathers.


Cernach son of Fergus, king of Loch Gabor, dies.


A hosting by Aed to Dún Cuair, and he divided Laigin between two Muiredachs,
i.e. Muiredach son of Ruaidrí, and Muiredach son of Bran.


The killing of Cormac son of Muirgius, abbot of Baislec; and the subsequent
devastation of Ciarraige by Muirgius.


Muirchertach son of Donngal, king of Bréifne, died.

U805.10

Cell Achaid, with its new oratory, caught fire.

# 806 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 805.


In which a great pestilence broke out in the island of Ireland.


Gormgal son of Dindathach, abbot of Ard Macha and Cluain Eóis, died.


The learned Congal son of Maenach, abbot of Sláine, fell asleep as a celibate.


Fínnechta son of Cellach, assumed his kingship again.




Patrick's law promulgated by Aed son of Niall.


A great crop of mast.


Loithech, teacher of Bennchor, rested.


The community of Í, to the number of sixty-eight, was killed by the heathens.


Connmach, judge of the Uí Briúin, dies.

U806.10

Flaithnia son of Cinaed, king of Uí Fhailgi, was killed in Ráith Imgáin.


Tír da Glas is burned.

# 807 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 806.


Connmach son of Dub dá Leithe, abbot of Ard Macha, died suddenly.


The slaying of Artgal son of Cathasach, king of Uí Chruinn of Airthir.


The killing of Conall son of Tadc, by Conall son of Aedacán in Cenn Tíre.


Building of the new monastery of Colum Cille at Cenannas.


Elarius, anchorite and scribe of Loch Cré, fell asleep.


The moon was turned to the colour of blood.


Murchad son of Flann, king of Uí Fhidgente, died.


The heathens burned Inis Muiredaig and invade Ros Comáin.


A battle between the community of Corcach and the community of Cluain Ferta
Brénainn, among whom resulted a slaughter of a countless number of ordinary
ecclesiastics and of eminent men of the community of Corcach.

# 808 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 807.


Death of Torbach alias Calbhach from Cluain Cracha, scribe and abbot of Ard
Macha.


Tomás, bishop, scribe and abbot of Linn Duachail, rested.


Faelgus, superior of Cell Achaid, fell asleep.


An expeditionary force of Muirgius son of Tomaltach, with the Connachtmen, was
led by Conchobur son of Donnchad as far as Tír ind Aenaig, and they hastened
away suddenly after three nights, and Aed son of Niall marched to oppose them,
and burned the borders of Mide; and their flight was likened to that of goats
and kids.




The killing of Cinaed son of Conchobor in Mag Cobo by the Cruithin.


Fínnechta son of Cellach, king of Laigin, died of haemorrhoids in Cell Dara.

# 809 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 808.


The falling asleep of Toictech alias Taichlech from Tír Imchláir, abbot of Ard
Macha.


A battle between the Uí Cheinnselaig themselves, in which Cellach Tosach son of
Donngal, king of Ráith Étain, fell.


The killing of Dúnchú, superior of Tulach Léis, beside the shrine of Patrick in
the abbot's house at Tulach Léis.


Baetán of Cluain Tuaiscirt, Cú Chiaráin, vice-abbot of Cluain, fell asleep.


Fire from heaven struck down a man in the oratory of Nuadu.


Finnbil, abbess of Cluain Brónaig, dies.


The invasion of Ulaid by Aed son of Niall, as a result of the profanation of
Patrick's shrine to the dishonour of Dúnchú.

	(The distressed but valiant nobles turn back
	The Ulaid had been cut off by Aed;
	There they remained discomfited
	At the fiery conflict of Bréne.
	The haughty unshaven horde
	Began to traverse the harbours;
	Birds' bills with bearded heads were seen
	Coming from the churches of Ulaid.)


Mael Fothartaig son of Flann, abbot of Finnubair Abae and Cell Moinni, died.


A battle among the Ulaid between two sons of Fiachna. Cairill was victor.
Echaid escaped.

# 810 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 809.


Cathnia, abbot of Dam Liac, and Tigernach, founder of Daire Meilli, abbot of
Cell Achaid, and Mael Fothartaig son of Aedgal, superior of Airecal Do-Chiaróc,
scribe, Cathal son of Fiachra, king of Ráith Airthir and Fir Chúl, and Echaid
son of Fiachna, king of Ulaid, and Mael Dúin son of Donngal,


steward of Ard Macha, Anluan son of Conchobor, king of Aidne, Mac Coirb son of
Neuter, and Cellach grandson of Cú Chodaig, died.


Guaire, abbot of Glenn dá Locha, fell asleep.


Tadc and Flaithnia, two sons of Muirgius, were killed by the Luigni.
Devastation of the Luigni by Muirgius.

	(Muirgius killed my son;
	Stupendous that it can befall me
	That it is I who plied a sword
	On Tadc's throat for it.

            A warrior of the Luigni of Connacht recited.)

# 811 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 810.


Nuadu, abbot of Ard Macha, went to Connacht with Patrick's law and his casket.


The fair of Tailtiu was prevented from being held on Saturday under the aegis
of Aed son of Niall, neither horse nor chariot arriving there. It was the
community of Tamlacht who caused the boycott after the Uí Néill had violated
the sanctuary of Tamlacht of Mael Ruain; and many gifts were subsequently made
to the community of Tamlacht.


Admaer, daughter of Aed of Laigin, died at a good old age.


Dímán of Ara, a Munster anchorite, ended his life happily.


Tuathgal, abbot of the elders of Cluain, died.


A slaughter of the heathens by the Ulaid.


Blathmac grandson of Muirdibor, abbot of Dairmag, died.

# 812 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 811.


Flann son of Cellach, abbot of Finnglas, a scribe, anchorite and bishop, died
suddenly.


Item: the bishop and anchorite Echaid, superior of Tamlacht, rested.


Coscrach son of Niallgus, king of Garbros, and Cernach son of Flaithnia, king
of Mugdorn of Brega, died.


Nuadu of Loch Uama, bishop, anchorite and abbot of Ard Macha, fell asleep.


Flann son of Congalach, king of Ciannacht, dies.


Aengus son of Dúnlang, king of Cenél Artgail, and Flaithbertach son of Cairpre,
superior of Cell Mór Enir, die.




Aed Rón, king of Corcu Baiscinn, dies.


A slaughter of the heathens by the men of Umall. A slaughter of the Conmaicne
by the heathens.


A slaughter of the Calraige of Lurg by the Uí Briúin.

U812.10

A slaughter of the Corcu Róidi of Mide by the Uí Moccu Uais.


A slaughter of the heathens in Mumu, viz. by Cobthach son of Mael Dúin, king of
Loch Léin.


The south was invaded by Muirgius son of Tomaltach, king of Connacht.


Daire's law promulgated in Connacht.

# 813 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 812.


Conall son of Daimthech, superior of Treóit, dies.


Feradach son of Scannal, a scribe, priest and abbot of Achad Bó, ended his life
happily.


Cellach son of Echaid, superior of Cell Tuama, dies.


The slaughter at Umall by the heathens in which fell Coscrach son of Flannabra
and Dúnadach, king of Umall.


Laegaire son of Cú Gamna, king of Cenél Cairpri, dies.


Congaltach son of Eitguin, prior of Cluain Ferta, dies.


Carolus, king of the Franks, or rather emperor of the whole of Europe, fell
asleep in peace.


Daire's law promulgated among the Uí Néill.

# 814 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 813.


Feidlimid, abbot of Cell Moinne, Patrick's steward of Brega, and a
distinguished anchorite and excellent scribe, ended his life happily.


Tuathal son of Du-Dubtae, a distinguished scribe and teacher of Cluain Moccu
Nóis, fell asleep.


Eiterscél son of Cellach, bishop of Glenn dá Locha, and Cinaed son of Cellach,
bishop and abbot of Trelec Mór, died.


Bishop Mael Dúin, superior of Echdruim, was killed.


Suibne son of Maenach, steward of Sláine, and Gormgal son of Niall son of
Fergal, died.


Muirghius and Fairchellach led an army across Uí Maini south of the Soc, and
many harmless people were killed.




Fairchellach of Fobar, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, and Orthanach, abbot of Cell
Fhoibric, and bishop Rónán grandson of Lóchderc, all fell asleep.


A battle between the Laigin themselves in which the Uí Cheinnselaig were
overthrown, and Bran's sons obtained the victory.


Cellach, abbot of Í, when the building of the church of Cenannas was finished,
resigned the office of superior, and Diarmait, fosterling of Daigre, was
appointed in his place.

U814.10

Braen son of Ruaidrí, a satrap of Laigin, dies.


Ciarán's law was exalted at Cruachain by Muirgius.


Great distress and severe illnesses.


Niall son of Aed, king of Uí Chormaic, dies unexpectedly.


Blathmac son of Aelgus, abbot of Tír dá Glas, and Blathmac fosterling of Colgu,
abbot of Inis Bó Finne, died.

# 815 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 814.


Death of Muirgius, king of Connacht.

	(A fiery red king without compassion,
	Muirgius from populous Cruachain
	Cellach's son of Cuirrech dispatched him
	North from the watery Berba.)


Céile Ísu, abbot of Cell Moinne, died.


The killing of Coscrach son of Fínnechta.


Conall son of Niall, king of southern Brega, dies.


Colmán son of Niall was killed by the Cenél Conaill. Aed afterwards made an
expedition against Cenél Conaill, on which Rogaillnech son of Flaithgus fell.


Mael Cánaig, anchorite of Lugmad, and Cellach son of Congal, abbot of Í, fell
asleep.


Cluain Crema was plundered, and people killed in it, by the men of Bréifne and
the Síl Cathail.


Fócarta son of Cernach, one of two kings of southern Brega, dies.



# 816 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 815.


Dúngal son of Cuanu, king of Ros, Tuathal son of Domnall, king of eastern Life,
Írgalach son of Mael Umai, king of Corcu Sogain, Conan son of Rhodri, king of
the Welsh, Cathal son of Artrí, king of Mugdorna, all died.


Dub dá Leithe son of Tomaltach, chief of Uí Maine(?), died.


Death of Ioseph, scribe of Ros Comáin.


The burning of almost half of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


Death of Suibne son of Cuanu, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis.


Cellach son of Muirgius, abbot of Druim Cara, was killed by Gerthide son of
Tuathal.


A great wind-storm on the Kalends 1st of November.


A battle was won over the Uí Fhiachrach of Muiresc by Diarmait son of
Tomaltach, and Foibrén in the territory of Grecraige was burned and sacked,
very many common people being killed there.


Death of Cathal son of Ailill, king of Uí Fhiachrach.

U816.10

The oratory of Fobar was burned.

# 817 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 816.


Death of Dathal grandson of Duibléne, a bishop, scribe and anchorite.


Death of the scribe Cú Chruithne, superior of Lann Ela.


Tipraite, abbot of Cluain Ferta Brénainn, and Cumuscach son of Cernach, steward
of Ard Macha, died.


A skirmish between the men of southern Brega and the Cianachta, in which many
of the Cianachta fell.


Cathal son of Dúnlang and the community of Tech Munna won a battle against the
community of Ferna, in which four hundred were slain.


Mael Tuile, abbot of Bennchor, goes into exile.


Mael Dúin son of Cenn Faelad, superior of Ráith Both, a member of Colum Cille's
community, was slain.


Colum Cille's community went to Temair to excommunicate Aed.


Mael Dúin, king of Ros, dies.

U817.10

Donngal son of Tuathal, king of Ard, dies.


Cilléne, abbot of Ferna, dies.


Fergus of Ráith Luraig, abbot of Finnglas, dies.


Siadal, abbot and bishop of Ros Comáin, fell asleep.



# 818 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 817.


Muiredach son of Bran, one of two kings of Laigin, dies.


There was abnormal ice and much snow from the Epiphany to Shrovetide. The Bóinn
and other rivers were crossed dry-footed; lakes likewise. Herds and
hunting-parties were on Loch nEchach, and wild deer were hunted. The materials
for an oratory were afterwards brought by a large company (?) from the lands of
Connacht over Upper and Lower Loch Éirne into Tír Ua Crimthainn; and other
unusual things were done in the frost and hail.


Cellach son of Scannlán, abbot of Cell Fhoibric, and Cernach son of Congalach,
king of Cnodba, die.


Cuanu, abbot of Lugmad, went into exile into the lands of Mumu with the shrine
of Mochta.


Artrí, superior of Ard Macha, went to Connacht with the shrine of Patrick.


Aed son of Niall went with forces to Dún Cuair and divided Laigin between the
two grandsons of Bran.


The superior of Cell Mór Enir was dishonoured, and Dub Innrecht, its prior, was
killed by the Laigin while under his protection.


Cellach son of Fogartach plundered a vessel (?) to the detriment of Conchobor
son of Muiredach, viz. the son of the king of Laigin.


A battle, i.e. the battle of Forath, was fought in the territory of Delbna
Nuadat, in which the Uí Maini with their king, i.e. Cathal son of Murchad, and
very many other nobles, were overthrown. The kings of the Uí Briúin, i.e.
Diarmait son of Tomaltach and Mael Cothaid son of Fogartach, were victors.

U818.10

Rechtabra grandson of Andola, abbot of Daiminis, dies.


A third part of Cluain Moccu Nóis was again burned on the twelfth of the
Kalends of November 21 October.

# 819 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 818.


The laying waste of Laigin by Aed son of Niall, i.e. the land of Cualu as far
as Glenn dá Locha.


Death of Aed son of Niall Aed the eminent son of Niall of the showers near Áth
dá Ferta in the plain of Conaille.




A skirmish between the Cenél Eógain and the Cenél Conaill in which Mael Bresail
son of Murchad, king of Cenél Conaill, fell. Murchad son of Mael Dúin was
victor.


A skirmish between the Ulaid themselves, in which Cairell son of Fiachna fell
and Muiredach son of Eochu was victor.


Cathal son of Dúnlang, king of Uí Cheinnselaig and prior of Ferna, dies.


Crunnmael son of Ailill, superior of Dam Liac, and Muiredach son of Crunnmael,
abbot of Dísert Teornóc, died.


Congalach son of Fergus, king of Fir Chúl, dies.


At Ard Macha Whitsun 5 June was not publicly celebrated nor the shrine taken on
tour; and there was a disturbance in which the son of Echaid son of Fiachna
fell.


Fothad of Othan dies.

# 820 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 819.


Dalach son of Congus, superior of Dam Liac, dies.


Murchad, with the northern Uí Néill, made an expedition to Druim ind Eich;
Conchobor with the southern Uí Néill and the Laigin moved northwards, till at
length God by his great power separated them.


Constantine son of Fergus, king of Fortriu, dies.


Mael Tuile, abbot of Bennchor, rested.


Feidlimid son of Crimthann took the kingship of Caisel.

# 821 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 820.


Crunnmael son of Odrán, abbot of Cluain Iraird, died.


Coenwulf, king of the Saxons, dies.


Étar was plundered by the heathens, and they carried off a great number of
women into captivity.


Cenn Faelad son of Rúman, scribe, bishop, anchorite and abbot of Áth Truim,
fell asleep.


Conchobor son of Donnchad made an expedition to Ardachad of Sliab Fuait. Int
Airthir was laid waste as far as Emain Macha.

# 822 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 821.


Mac Riaguil grandson of Magléne, scribe, bishop and abbot of Biror, died.


Abnormal ice; the seas, lakes and rivers froze and herds of horses and cattle,
and loads, were brought across them.




Murchad son of Mael Dúin made an expedition with the men of the North to Ard
Brecáin. The men of Brega, i.e. Diarmait son of Niall and the descendants of
Aed Sláine, then went over secretly to Murchad and submitted to him at Druim
Fergusa. Conchobor son of Donnchad invaded Brega and camped at Gualu. He
invaded southern Brega again on the Kalends 1st of November, and a vast number
of the men of southern Brega fell by him, and the Uí Chernaig submitted under
compulsion.


Death of Forbasach abbot of Achad Bó Cainnig.


Cumuscach son of Tuathal, king of Ard Ciannachta, was killed by Murchad.


The battle of Tarbga between the Connachta themselves. The Uí Briúin were
overthrown and very many nobles were slain, including the leaders, Dúnchad son
of Maenach, and Gormgal son of Dúnchad. The Uí Maini and Diarmait son of
Tomaltach were victors.


A slaughter of the men of Bréifne, including their king, i.e. Mael Dúin son of
Échtgal, was inflicted by the Cenél Feidilmthe.


The men of Ard Cianacht, were routed by Cumuscach son of Congalach, and Eudus
son of Tigernach and many others fell.


Eochu grandson of Tuathal, anchorite, bishop and abbot of Lugbad, fell asleep.

U822.10

The Uí Garbáin, the Cuircne, and the Felle routed the Delbna.

# 823 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 822.


Diarmait son of Donnchad, abbot of Ros Ech, died.


Dub dá Crích son of Mael Tuile, abbot of Cell Achaid, fell asleep.


Sechnasach of Loch Cendin, bishop and anchorite, rested.


Conaing son of Congal, king of Tethba, dies.


Patrick's law was imposed on Mumu by Feidlimid son of Crimthann, and by Artri
son of Conchobor i.e. bishop of Ard Macha.


Rónán, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, resigned his office as superior.


Murchad son of Mael Dúin was deposed by Niall son of Aed and the Cenél Eógain.




Heathens invaded Bennchor the great.


Galinne of the Britons with all its dwelling-places and oratory was burned by
Feidlimid.

U823.10

Fire from heaven struck the abbot's mansion in Ard Macha and burned it.

# 824 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 823.


Niall son of Fergus, chief of Uí Fhorannáin, dies.


The heathens plundered Bennchor at Airtiu (?), and destroyed the oratory, and
shook the relics of Comgall from their shrine.

	(It will be true, true,
	By the will of the High-king of kings,
	My bones shall be borne without harm
	From Bennchor of the fighting to Aentreb.)


A battle, i.e. the battle of Finnubair, between the men of Tethba themselves,
in which Aed son of Fogartach and many others fell.


The greater part of Ros Comáin was burned.


A battle between the Connachta themselves in which very many fell.


A skirmish between Dúnchad and Cumuscach, two kings of Cianacht, in which many
were slain. Dúnchad was victor. Cumuscach escaped by flight.


Eochaid son of Bresal, king of Dál Araidi of the North, was killed by his
associates.


Spelán son of Sluagadach, king of Conaille of Muirtheimne, dies.


Étgal of Scelec was carried off by the heathens, and died shortly afterwards of
hunger and thirst.

# 825 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 824.


Cuanu of Lugbad, a scholar and a bishop, fell asleep.


Diarmait grandson of Aed Róin, anchorite and teacher of religion for all
Ireland, died; also Cuimnech, abbot of Finnglas.


Aedán, abbot of Tamlacht, and Flannabra, superior of Mag Bile, die.


Colmán son of Ailill, abbot of Sláine and of other monasteries in France and
Ireland, died.




Fergal son of Cathrannach, king of Loch Riach, dies.


Mael Bresail son of Ailill of Cuib, king of Dál Araidi, dies.


A great pestilence in the island of Ireland affected the old, the children and
the weak; there was great famine and shortage of bread.


Aengus son of Mael Dúin, king of Loch Gabor, dies.


Dún Lethglaise was plundered by the heathens.

U825.10

Mag Bile with its oratories was burned by the heathens.


The Ulaid inflicted a rout on the heathens in Mag Inis, in which very many
fell.


The heathens inflicted a rout on the Osraige.


Flann son of Fairchellach, abbot of Les Mór, fell asleep.


Patrick's law was imposed on the three Connachta by Artrí

son of Conchobor, bishop of Ard Macha.


The plundering of Inis Daimle by the heathens.


Fallomon son of Fogartach was killed by his own kinsman, called Cellach.


The violent death of Blamac son of Flann at the hands of the heathens in Í
Coluim Chille.

# 826 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 825.


Diarmait son of Niall, king of southern Brega, dies.


Niall son of Diarmait, king of Mide, died.


Loingsech's son, abbot of Ard Macha, died peacefully.


Art son of Diarmait, king of Tethba, was deceitfully killed.


Bishop Clemens, abbot of Cluain Iraird, ended his life happily.


Great terror in all Ireland, i.e. from a warning of plague given by Iellán's
son of Mumu.


Mael Dúin son of Gormgal, king of Uí Méith, died in clerical life.


The burning of Delbna Bethra by Feidlimid. He had a Munster army.


The mortal wounding of Artrí son of Muirgius, king of Tethba.

U826.10

Daire's law was brought to Connacht again.


Ruithnél, superior and bishop of Cluain Ferta Brénainn, dies.



# 827 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 826.


Échtgus, superior of Tamlacht, fell asleep.


The dishonouring of Eogan in Ard Macha by Cumuscach son of Cathal and by Artrí
son of Conchobor.


Lusca was plundered by the heathens and burned; and Cianacht was invaded as far
as Uachtar Ugán; and also a plundering of the foreigners of the East.


The battle of Leth Cam won by Niall son of Aed against the Uí Chremthainn and
Muiredach son of Echaid, king of Ulaid, in which fell Cumuscach and Congalach,
two sons of Cathal, and many other kings of the Airgialla.


A disturbance of the Fair of Tailtiu caused by an attack on the Gailenga by
Conchobor son of Donnchad, and many fell therein.


A disturbance of the Fair of Colmán caused by an attack on the Laigin Desgabair
by Muiredach, and very many fell therein.


Maenach son of Crunnmael, prior of Fir Rois, dies.


Abniér, abbot of Cell Achaid, fell asleep.


An encampment of the Laigin was overwhelmed by the heathens, and Conall son of
Cú Chongalt, king of the Fortuatha, and countless others fell there.

U827.10

A royal conference at Biror between Feidlimid and Conchobor.

# 828 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 827.


Uatha son of Diarmait, king of Tethba, was killed.


Robartach son of Cathasach, superior of Cluain Mór of Ard, Muirchiu, abbot of
Druim Inasclainn, and Clemens, abbot of Linn Duachaill, fell asleep.


A great slaughter of porpoises on the coast of Ard Cianachta by the foreigners;
and the violent death of the anchorite Teimnén.


The mortal wounding of Cinaed son of Cumuscach, king of Ard Cianachta, by the
foreigners; and Lann Léire and Cluain Mór were burned by them.


A battle-rout was inflicted on the heathens by Lethlobar son of Loingsech, king
of Dál Araidi.


Another battle-rout was inflicted on the heathens by Cairpre son of Cathal,
king of Uí Cheinnselaig, and by the community of Tech Munnu.


A slaughter of the Delbna in Felle.



# 829 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 828.


Muiredach son of Ruaidrí, king of Laigin, Aed son of Cellach, superior of Cell
Dara, Mael Dobarchon, abbot of Cell Usaile, Cinaed son of Mugrón, king of Uí
Fhailgi, Cormac son of Muirgius, superior of Sentrab, Cerball son of Fínnechta,
king of Delbna, Mael Umai son of Ceithernach, steward of Finnubair, Drucán son
of Tadc, king of Uí Méith all died.


Conaing son of Cellach was deceitfully killed by Eochu son of Cernach.


Diarmait, abbot of Í, went to Scotland with the halidoms of Colum Cille.


A battle-rout of the Connachta by the men of Mide, in which many fell.

# 830 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 829.


Aengus son of Donnchad, king of Telcha Mide, dies.


Cormac son of Suibne, abbot of Cluain Iraird, a scribe and bishop, rested in
peace.


Cellach son of Cú Chothraige, superior of Achad Crimthainn, dies.


The burning of Foire by Feidlimid.


Folloman son of Donnchad was killed by the Munstermen.


The smashing of the southern Uí Briúin by Feidlimid.


Ioseph son of Nechtain, abbot of Ros Comáin, rested.


Fínnechta son of Bodbchad, king of Cenél Meic Erca, died.


Suibne son of Fairnech alias son of Forannán abbot of Ard Macha for two months,
died.

# 831 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 830.


Diarmait came back to Ireland with the halidoms of Colum Cille.


Muirenn, abbess of Cell Dara, fell asleep.


Airmedach, superior of Mag Bile, was drowned.


Cernach son of Dunchú, scribe, man of learning, and priest of Ard Macha,
rested.


The fair of Tailtiu was disturbed at the platforms owing to dissension over the
shrine of MacCuilinn and the halidoms of Patrick, and many died as a result.


Conaille was invaded by the heathens, and Mael


Brigte, its king, and his brother Canannán, were taken prisoner and taken away
to the ships.


The heathens defeated the community of Ard Macha in a battle at Aignig, and
great numbers of them were taken captive.


Eógan of Mainister abbot of Ard Macha was dishonoured over a legal decision (?)
by Conchobor son of Donnchad, and his followers were taken prisoner, and his
horses taken away.


Feidlimid son of Crimthann, with an army from Mumu and Laigin, came to Fiambur
to plunder the men of Brega.

U831.10

The plundering of Life by Conchobor.

# 832 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 831.


The first plundering of Ard Macha by the heathens three times in one month.


The plundering of Mucnám, Lugbad, Uí Méith, Druim Moccu Blae, and other
churches.


The plundering of Dam Liac and the sept of the Cianacht with all their churches
by the heathens.


Ailill son of Colgu was taken captive by the heathens.


Tuathal son of Feradach was taken away by the heathens, and Adamnán's shrine
from Domnach Maigen.


Ráith Luraig and Connaire were plundered by the heathens.


Cinaed son of Echaid, king of Dál Araidi of the North, was deceitfully killed
by his own associates.


Cinaed son of Artrí, king of Cualu, and Diarmait son of Ruaidrí, king of
eastern Life, died.


Conchobor son of Donnchad, king of Ireland, died.

# 833 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 832.


Artrí son of Conchobor, abbot of Ard Macha, and Conchobor son of Donnchad, king
of Temair, died in the same month.


Ruaidrí son of Mael Fothartach, one of two kings of Uí Chremthainn, dies.


Niall of the Calann begins to reign.


Niall and Murchad routed the foreigners in Daire Calgaig.


Cluain Dolcáin was plundered by the heathens.




Cellach son of Bran routed the community of Cell Dara in a battle in their
monastery, many being killed, on St. John's day in the autumn 29 Aug..


Feidlimid, king of Caisel, put to death members of the community of Cluain
Moccu Nóis and burned their church-lands to the very door of their church. The
community of Dairmag were treated likewise to the very door of their church.


Death of Diarmait son of Tomaltach, king of Connacht.


Death of Cobthach son of Mael Dúin, king of Iarmumu.

U833.10

The burning of Les Mór Mo-Chutu and a slaughter of the people of Desmumu.


Druim Inasclainn was burned by the heathens.


Loch Bricrenn was plundered to the detriment of Congalach son of Echaid, and he
was killed afterwards at the ships.


Rechtabra, abbot of Cell Achaid, died.


Tipraite son of Ruamlus, superior of Domnach Sechnaill, dies.

# 834 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 833.


Aengus son of Fergus, king of Foirtriu, dies.


Eógan of Mainister, abbot of Ard Macha and Cluain Iraird, and Affraic, abbess
of Cell Dara, fell asleep.


Cellach son of Bran, king of Laigin, and Cinaed son of Conaing, king of Tethba,
died.

         1. (A sorrowful tale overcomes my heart,

            [...]
            man(?)
            [...]
            tear(s) of blood,
            Cinaed of Corann has been slain,
            The son of Conaing, the head of Bruig.)


Suibne son of Artrí, king of all Mugdorna, was killed by his kinsmen.


Conchobor son of Ailill was killed by his kinsmen.


Congalach son of Aengus, king of Cenél Laegaire, dies.


Tuathchar, bishop and scribe of Cell Dara, died.


Dúnadach son of Scannlán, king of Uí Fhidgeinte, won a battle against the
heathens, in which many fell.




Glenn dá Locha was plundered by the heathens. Sláine and Finnubair Abae were
plundered by the heathens.

U834.10

Nearly half of Cluain Moccu Nóis was burned.


Brócán son of Céndercán, was mortally wounded in Aidne.

# 835 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 834.


Niall led an army to Laigin and he set up a king over the Laigin, i.e. Bran son
of Faelán.


Cinaed son of Niall son of Aed was killed by the Ulaid.


Niall invaded Mide and burned the country as far as Tech Maíl Chonóc.


Cumuscach son of Aengus, prior of Cluain Moccu Nóis, dies.


Ferna and Cluain Mór Maedóc were plundered by the heathens.


A change of abbots in Ard Macha, i.e. Forannán from Ráith Meic Malais instead
of Diarmait grandson of Tigernán.


Echaid son of Cú Chongalt, king of Uí Thuirtri, and Caenchomrac son of Siadal,
steward of Cell Dara, and Bresal son of Cormac, superior of Cell Dumai Glinn
and other monasteries, and Muirchertach son of Gormgal, all died.


Fergus son of Bodbchad, king of Carraic Brachaide, was killed by Munstermen.


Death of Dúnadach son of Scannlán, king of Uí Fhidgente.

U835.10

A third part of Cluain Moccu Nóis was burned on the second of the nones 6th of
March.


Mungairit and other churches of Iarmumu were burned by the heathen.


The foreigners plundered Druim Ing.

# 836 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 835.


Suibne son of Ioseph, abbot of Glenn dá Locha, Saergus grandson of Cuinnid,
abbot of Dairmag, Forbasach, bishop and anchorite of Lusca, all ended their
life happily.


Dúnlang son of Cathusach, superior of Corcach Mór, died without communion in
Caisel of the kings.


The oratory of Cell Dara was seized by Feidlimid by force of arms from
Forannán, abbot of Ard Macha, and the congregation of Patrick; and they were
disrespectfully blockaded.




Diarmait went to Connachta with Patrick's law and his insignia.


Cell Dara was plundered by heathens from Inber Dea, and half of the church was
burned.


Cairpre son of Mael Dúin, king of Loch Gabor, was killed by Mael Cerna, and
Mael Cerna was killed by the Cairpri within the hour; and both died the same
night.


The first prey was taken by the heathens from southern Brega, i.e. from Telcha
Dromáin and Dairmag of the Britons; and they carried off many prisoners, and
killed many and led away very many captive.


A great crop of mast, both nuts and acorns, and as a result the streams became
blocked up and stopped running.


The battle of Drong between the Connachta themselves in which fell Cellach son
of Forbasach, superior of Ros Cam, and Adamnán son of Alddail; and Connmach the
great was victor.

U836.10

A most cruel devastation of all the lands of Connacht by the heathens. The
heathens inflicted a slaughter in a battle won over the Déis Tuaisceirt.

# 837 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 836.


The bishop and anchorite Flaithroa, abbot of Mainister Buiti, and Martain,
bishop of Cluain Chaín, rested.


Mael Dúin son of Sechnasach, king of Fir Chúl, Tuathal son of Fiangalach, king
of Cenél Ardgail, Riacán son of Fínnechta, one of two kings of the Laigin,
died.


A naval force of the Norsemen sixty ships strong was on the Bóinn, and another
one of sixty ships on the river Life. Those two forces plundered the plain of
Life and the plain of Brega, including churches, forts and dwellings. The men
of Brega routed the foreigners at Deoninne in Mugdorna of Brega, and six score
of the Norsemen fell.


The heathens won a battle at Inber na mBárc against the Uí Néill from the
Sinann to the sea, in which an uncounted number were slaughtered, though the
principal kings escaped.


Inis Celtra was plundered by the heathens.


The churches of all Loch Éirne, including Cluain Eóis and Daiminis, were
destroyed by the heathens.


Cenél Cairpri Cruim was plundered by Feidlimid.


Cathal son of Muirgius inflicted a rout on the Munstermen.


Saxolb, chief of the foreigners, was killed by the Cianacht.



# 838 A.D.

Kalends of January. AD 837.


Cormac, bishop and scribe of Cell Foibric, Bran, bishop and scribe of Finnglas,
Tigernach son of Aed, abbot of Finnubair Abae and other monasteries, fell
asleep.


Domnall son of Aed, superior of Druim Urchaille, dies.


Cellach son of Coscrach, superior of Airecul Ciaróc, and Cellach son of
Cairpre, superior of Áth Truim, died.


Congalach son of Maenach, king of Uí Moccu Uais of Brega, ended his life by a
sudden death.


Mael Crón, i.e. son of Cobthach, king of Loch Léin, dies.


A great royal conference in Cluain Chonaire Tómáin, between Feidlimid and
Niall.


Do-Chutu, a holy bishop and anchorite of Sláine, ended his life happily in old
age.


Ferdálach, steward of Ard Macha, died.


The heathens won a battle against the Connachta, in which Mael Dúin son of
Muirgius and many others fell.

U838.10

Bran son of Faelán, king of Laigin, dies.

# 839 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 838.


Mael Gaimrid, an excellent scribe and anchorite, and abbot of Bennchor, rested.


Colmán son of Robartach, abbot of Sláine, Aedán abbot of Ros Cré, Cormac son of
Conall, superior of Treóit, Mael Ruanaid son of Cathasach, prior of Lusca,
died.


Cumascach son of Congalach, king of Cianachta, dies.


Muiredach son of Echaid, king of Conchobor's Province, was killed by his own
kinsmen, i.e. by Aed and Aengus, and many others.


Cennétig son of Congalach, king of Uí Moccu Uais of Brega, was deceitfully
killed by his own kinsman, i.e. Céile.


Crunnmael son of Fiannamail, steward of Dairmag, was killed by Mael Sechnaill,
son of Mael Ruanaid.


A raiding party of the foreigners were on Loch nEchach, and from there they
plundered the states and churches of the north of Ireland.


A change of abbots in Ard Macha, i.e. Diarmait grandson of Tigernach, instead
of Forannán from Ráith Meic Malais.




The heathens won a battle against the men of Foirtriu, and Eóganán son of
Aengus, Bran son of Óengus, Aed son of Boanta, and others almost innumerable
fell there.

U839.10

Ferna and Corcach were burned by the heathens.

# 840 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 839.


Lugbad was plundered by the heathens from Loch nEchach and they led away
captive bishops and priests and scholars, and put others to death.


Floriacus, emperor of the Franks, dies.


Ard Macha was burned with its oratories and stone church.


Feidlimid king of Mumu invaded Mide and Brega, and halted at Temair; and on
that occasion Fir Chell and Bethra were invaded by Niall son of Aed.

         1. (Feidlimid is the king
            For whom a single day's work is
            To take the hostages of Connacht without battle
            And to spoil Mide.)


Death of Murchad son of Aed, king of Connacht.


Cinaed son of Coscrach, king of Bregmaine, was fatally wounded in Tethba.


Ioseph of Ros Mór, bishop, excellent scribe, anchorite, and abbot of Cluain
Eóis and other monasteries, fell asleep.


In this year below the Norsemen first came to Ireland, according to the senchus
.

U841.O

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 840.


The heathens were still on Loch nEchach.


Diarmait son of Conchobor subjected Mael Ruanaid son of Donnchad to compulsion
(?), and Diarmait was later killed by Mael Sechnaill on the same day, and Mael
Ruanaid was left alive.


Aed son of Dúnchad was deceitfully killed by the associates of Conaing son of
Flann in the latter's presence.


There was a naval camp at Linn Duachaill from which the peoples and churches of
Tethba were plundered. There was a naval camp at Duiblinn from which the Laigin
and the Uí Néill were plundered, both states and churches, as far as Sliab
Bladma.




Feidlimid led an army as far as Carmain. Niall marched against him to Mag
Óchtar.

         1. (The crozier of devout Feidlimid
            Was abandoned in the blackthorns;
            Niall, mighty in combat, took it
            By right of victory in battle with swords.)

# 842 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 841.


Muiredach son of Cernach, steward of Ard Macha, dies.


The heathens still at Duiblinn.


Fínnechta son of Bresal, abbot of Cell Dumai Glinn, and Cumsud son of Ruamlus,
abbot of Domnach Sechnaill all died.


Feidlimid of Cell Mór Enir, a bishop, rested.


Mael Dúin son of Conall, king of Calatruim, was taken prisoner by the heathens.


Cluain Moccu Nóis was plundered by heathens from Linn Duachaill.


Biror and Saiger were plundered by heathens from Duiblinn.


A naval force of the Norsemen was on the Bóinn at Linn Rois. There was also a
naval force of the Norsemen at Linn Sailech in Ulaid.


Mórán son of Indrechtach, abbot of Clochar Mac nDaiméni, was taken prisoner by
the foreigners of Linn, and later died on their hands.

U842.10

Comán, abbot of Linn Duachail, was fatally wounded and burned by heathens and
Irish.


Dísert Diarmata was plundered by heathens from Cael Uisci.


Cellach son of Cathgein, abbot of Druim Mór in Uí Echach, fell asleep.


Dúngall son of Fergal, king of Osraige, dies.

# 843 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 842.


Death of Mael Ruanaid son of Donnchad, i.e. king of Mide and father of Mael
Sechnaill.


Death of Cathal son of Conchobor.


Artacán son of Domnall was deceitfully killed by Ruarc son of Bran.


Cinaed son of Cú Raí, king of Cenél Laegaire, was killed by the Delbna.




Cumsud son of Dereir, and Maenach son of Sothchadach, two bishops and
anchorites, died on the same night in Dísert Diarmata.


Fergus son of Fothaid, king of Connacht, dies.


Donnacán son of Mael Tuile, scribe and anchorite, rested in Italy.


Suibne son of Forannán, abbot of Imlech Fia, dies.


Colgu son of Fedach, an anchorite, rested.

# 844 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 843.


Mael Mithig son of Cinaed was killed by the heathens.


Rónán, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, fell asleep.


Bricéne, abbot of Lothra, died.


Cluain Ferta Brénainn was burned by heathens from Loch Rí.

# 845 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 844.


Forannán, abbot of Ard Macha, was taken prisoner by the heathens in Cluain
Comarda with his halidoms and following, and was brought to the ships of
Luimnech.


Dún Masc was plundered by the heathens, and there were killed there Aed son of
Dub dá Crích, abbot of Tír dá Glas, and Cluain Eidnig, Ceithernach son of Cú
Dínaisc, prior of Cell Dara, and many others.


There was an encampment of the foreigners i.e. under Tuirgéis on Loch Rí, and
they plundered Connacht and Mide, and burned Cluain Moccu Nóis with its
oratories, and Cluain Ferta Brénainn, and Tír dá Glas and Lothra and other
monasteries.


Fiachna son of Mael Bresail, abbot of Finnubair Abae, dies.


Gormgal son of Muiredach, bishop and anchorite of Lann Léire, rested.


Niall son of Aed inflicted a battle-rout on the heathens in Mag Ítha.


Donnchad son of Follaman and Flann son of Mael Ruanaid were killed by Mael
Sechnaill son of Mael Ruanaid.


Tuirgéis was taken prisoner by Mael Sechnaill and afterwards drowned in Loch
Uair.


Labraid son of Ailill, abbot of Sláine, dies.



U845.10

Robartach son of Bresal, abbot of Achad Bó Cainnig; dies.


Robartach son of Flann, abbot of Domnach Mór, dies.


An encampment of the foreigners of Áth Cliath at Cluain Andobuir.

# 846 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 845.


Cathal son of Ailill, king of Uí Maini, Ferdomnach, scholar and excellent
scribe of Ard Macha, and Connmach the Great son of Coscrach, king of Uí Briúin,
fell asleep.


Baislec was plundered by the heathens.


Niall son of Aed, king of Temair, died by drowning i.e. at Linne Néill on the
Calann.

         1. (I love not the gloomy waters
            Which flow past my dwelling:
            O Calann, though you pray boast of it,
            You have drowned the son of an affectionate woman.)


Mael Dúin son of Conall, king of Calatruim, was killed by the Laigin.


Niall son of Cenn Faelad, king of Uí Fhidgente, dies.


The foreigners won a battle against the Connachta, in which fell Rígán son of
Fergus, Mugrón son of Diarmait and Aed son of Cathrannach and many others.


Tigernach inflicted a rout on Mael Sechnaill and Ruarc, in which many were
slaughtered.


Muiredach son of Flann, abbot of Mainister Buiti, died.


Forannán, abbot of Ard Macha, came from the lands of Mumu with the halidoms of
Patrick.

U846.10

Cairpre son of Colmán, abbot of Áth Truim, died.


Conaing son of Ferdomnach, abbot of Domnach Pátraic, died.


(Cathal son of Ailill, king of Uí Maini, died.)



# 847 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 846.


Feidlimid son of Crimthann, king of Mumu, a scribe and anchorite, and the best
of the Irish, rested.


(Mael Sechnaill son of Mael Ruanaid begins to reign.)


Mael Sechnaill destroyed the Island of Loch Muinremor, overcoming there a large
band of wicked men of Luigni and Gailenga, who had been plundering the
territories in the manner of the heathens.


Cerball son of Dúngal inflicted a great rout on Agnonn, in which twelve hundred
fell.


Mael Goán son of Echaid, king of Cenél Bógaine, died.


Cellach son of Mael Pátraic, prior of Fir Rois to the south of the river, dies.


Connmach son of Cernach, one of two kings of the Ciarraige of Connacht, died.


Artur son of Muiredach, king of western Life, died.


Cathal son of Coscrach, king of Fotharta, was killed by the Uí Néill.

# 848 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
AD 847.


A great snowfall on the Kalends 1st of February.


Fínnechta of Luibnech, anchorite and formerly king of Connacht, died.


Tuathchar son of Cobthach, king of Luigni, died.


Mael Sechnaill won a battle against the heathens at Forach in which seven
hundred fell.


Ólchobor, king of Mumu, and Lorcán son of Cellach, with the Laigin, won a
battle against the heathens at Sciath Nechtain, in which fell the jarl Tomrair,
tanist of the king of Lochlann, and two hundred about him.


Tigernach inflicted a rout on the heathens in the oakwood of Dísert Do-Chonna,
and twelve hundred fell there.


The Eóganacht of Caisel inflicted a rout on the heathens at Dún Maíle Tuile, in
which five hundred fell.


A change of abbots at Ard Macha, i.e. Diarmait instead of Forannán.


Diarmait of Cell Chan fell asleep.



# 849 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 848.


Conaing son of Flann, king of Brega, dies.


Cairpre son of Cinaed, king of Uí Maíl, dies.


Fínnechta son of Diarmait, abbot of Dam Liac, Mael Fuataig, abbot of Ard
Brecáin, Onchú, bishop and anchorite of Sláine, died.


Ailill son of Cumuscach, king of Loch Cal, dies.


Flaithbertach son of Céilechair was killed by his own kinsmen.


A naval expedition of seven score ships of adherents of the king of the
foreigners came to exact obedience from the foreigners who were in Ireland
before them, and afterwards they caused confusion in the whole country.


Indrechtach, abbot of Í, came to Ireland with the halidoms of Colum Cille.


Robartach son of Colgu, abbot of Sláine, went into voluntary exile.


Flannacán son of Echaid, king of Dál Araidi of the North, was killed by the
Cenél Eógain.

U849.10

Mael Bresail son of Cernach, king of Mugdorna, was killed by the heathens after
he had changed to clerical life.


Airennán, abbot of Bennchor, fell asleep.


(Mael Sechnaill's siege in Crupat.)

# 850 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 849.


Cétadach, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, and Tuathal son of Feradach, abbot of
Rechru and Dairmag, and Ferchar son of Muiredach, superior of Lann Léire, died.


Aengus son of Suibne, king of Mugdorna, was killed by Gairbíth son of Mael
Brígte.


Cinaed son of Conaing, king of Cianacht, rebelled against Mael Sechnaill with
the support of the foreigners, and plundered the Uí Néill from the Sinann to
the sea, both churches and states, and he deceitfully sacked the island of Loch
Gabor, levelling it to the ground, and the oratory of Treóit, with seventy
people in it, was burned by him.


Braen son of Ruaidrí, king of Uí Chremthainn, and his two brothers, Fogartach
and Bruatur, were killed by their own kinsmen.



# 851 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 850.


Colgu son of Cellach, superior of Cell Tuama, Scannal son of Tipraite, superior
of Domnach Sechnaill, and Ólchobor i.e. son of Cinaed, king of Caisel, died.


Cinaed son of Conaing, king of Cianacht, was cruelly drowned in a pool by Mael
Sechnaill and Tigernach, in spite of the guarantees of the nobles of Ireland,
and the successor of Patrick in particular.

         1. (Alas, o nobles,
            Better were his sportive days as a child;
            It is great grief that Conaing son of Cinaed
            Should be taken bound to a pit.)


The dark heathens came to Áth Cliath, made a great slaughter of the fair-haired
foreigners, and plundered the naval encampment, both people and property. The
dark heathens made a raid at Linn Duachaill, and a great number of them were
slaughtered.


Congalach son of Írgalach, king of Caille Follomain, dies.


A royal conference in Ard Macha, between Mael Sechnaill, accompanied by the
nobles of Leth Cuinn, and Matudán with the nobles of Conchobor's Province, and
Diarmait and Fethgna with the congregation of Patrick, and Suairlech with the
clerics of Mide.


Cairell son of Ruaidrí, king of Loch Uaithne, was deceitfully killed by the
Conaille of Fernmag before the oratory of Tigernach in Cluain Eóis.


Eochu son of Cernach, king of Fir Rois, was killed by the heathens.


Tipraite grandson of Baíthenach, abbot of Les Mór, fell asleep.

# 852 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 851.


Two successors of Patrick two bishops of Ard Macha, i.e. Forannán, scribe,
bishop and anchorite, and Diarmait, the most learned of all the teachers of
Europe, rested.


Ard Macha was laid waste by the foreigners of Linn on the day following
Summer-Lent.


The complement of eight score ships of fair-haired foreigners came to Snám
Aignech, to do battle with the dark foreigners; they fought for three days and
three nights, but the dark foreigners got the upper hand and the others
abandoned their ships to them. Stain took flight, and escaped, and Iercne fell
beheaded.




Maengal abbot of Ard Sratha, and Cenn Faelad, son of Ultán, scholar of Both
Conais, and Lergal, superior of Othan, fell asleep.


Fogartach son of Mael Bresail, king of Airgialla, dies.


Cathal son of Dubán, king of Uí Duach of Argatros, dies.


Forbasach son of Maelodar, superior of Cell Mór of Cenn Eich, dies.


A slaughter was inflicted on the foreigners at the islands of eastern Brega,
and another slaughter of them at Ráith Alláin by the Cianacht in the same
month.

# 853 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 852.


Ailill son of Robartach, superior of Lusca, and Flann son of Rechtabra, abbot
of Liath Mancháin, and Ailgenán son of Donngal, king of Caisel, died.


Amlaíb, son of the king of Lochlann, came to Ireland, and the foreigners of
Ireland submitted to him, and he took tribute from the Irish.


Echtigern son of Guaire, king of Laigin Desgabair, was deceitfully killed by
Bruatur son of Aed and by Cerball son of Dúngal; and Bruatur son of Aed was
deceitfully killed by his own associates on the eighth day after the killing of
Echtigern.


Flaithnia, abbot of Biror and a bishop, died.


Cernach son of Mael Bresail, king of Cuib, dies.


Cathmal son of Tomaltach, one of two kings of Ulaid, was killed by the
Norsemen.

# 854 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 853.


Tuathal son of Mael Brigte, king of Uí Dunlaing, was wickedly slain by his own
kinsmen.


Mael Sechnaill, king of Temair, marched against the men of Mumu to Inneóin na
nDéise, and took hostages from them.


The successor of Colum Cille, an excellent scholar, suffered a violent death
among the Saxons on the fourth of the Ides 12th of March.


Tigernach and Flann son of Conaing both raided Domnach Mór, but Flann gained
the day.

# 855 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 854.


Cathan, abbess of Cell Dara, dies.


There was snow to the height of men's belts on the ninth of the Kalends of May
23 April.




Aed son of Niall made a foray against the Ulaid, and he left behind dead
Coinnecán son of Colmán and Flaithbertach son of Niall, and a large number
besides.


Fínnechta i.e. son of Mael Brigte, was treacherously killed.


Ruidgus son of Maicnia, abbot of Mainister Buiti, was drowned.


Ailill, abbot of Achad Bó, Robartach, abbot of Inis Cáin Dega and a scribe, and
Muiredach, king of Ard Cianachta, die.

# 856 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 855.


There was much ice and frost so that the principal lakes and rivers of Ireland
could be crossed by people on foot and on horse back from the ninth of the
Kalends of December 23 Nov. to the seventh of the Ides 7 of January.


Mael Sechnaill, son of Mael Ruanaid, was in Caisel and he took hostages from
Mumu.


Great warfare between the heathens and Mael Sechnaill, supported by
Norse-Irish.


The oratory of Lusca was burned by the Norsemen.


Aed son of Niall inflicted a great rout on the Norse-Irish in Glenn Foichle and
a vast number of them were slaughtered by him.


Horm, chief of the dark foreigners, was killed by Rhodri son of Mervyn, king of
Wales.


Suibne grandson of Roichlech, scribe, anchorite and abbot of Les Mór, and
Cormac of Lathrach Briúin, a scribe and bishop, fell asleep in peace.


Sodomna, bishop of Sláine, suffers a violent death.

# 857 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 856.


Ímar and Amlaíb inflicted a rout on Caitil the Fair and his Norse-Irish in the
lands of Munster.


Maengal, abbot of Fobar, and Siadal of Dísert Ciaráin, rested.


Matudán son of Muiredach, king of Ulaid, dies.


Three persons were burned in Tailtiu by fire from heaven.


A great windstorm caused a destruction of trees and ruined lake islands.
Cellach recited:

         1. Since the fair fateful synod
            Of Nicea was held on noble matters
            This is the third year (not for untruth's sake)
            And thirty and five hundred.



# 858 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 857.


The bishop and anchorite Cumsud, superior of Cluain Iraird, rested in peace.


Cinaed son of Ailpín, king of the Picts, and Ethelwulf, king of the Saxons,
died.


Tipraite the Pale, abbot of Tír dá Glas, died.


Mael Sechnaill son of Mael Ruanaid came with the men of Ireland to the lands of
Mumu and halted for ten nights at Niam; and he plundered them to the south as
far as the sea, after their kings had been defeated at Carn Lugdach, Maelchrón
son of Muiredach, king of the Déisi, being left dead there. Mael Sechnaill then
took the hostages of Mumu from Belat Gabráin to Inis Tarbnai off the Irish
coast, and from Dún Cermna to Ára Airthir.


A rainy autumn destructive to the fruits of the earth.

# 859 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 858.


Suairlech, abbot of Achad Bó, Ailill of Banbán, abbot of Biror, Mael Coba
grandson of Faelán, abbot of Cluain Uama, and Faelgus, abbot of Ros Cré, fell
asleep in peace.


Amlaíb and Ímar and Cerball led a great army into Mide.


A royal conference was held at Ráith Aeda Meic Bric of the nobles of Ireland,
including Mael Sechnaill, king of Temair, and Fethgna, successor of Patrick,
and Suairlech, successor of Finnia, to make peace and amity between the men of
Ireland, and as a result of that meeting Cerball, king of Osraige, gave his
full dues to Patrick's congregation and his successor, and the Osraige were
alienated to Leth Cuinn, and Mael Guala, king of Mumu, warranted the
alienation.


Mael Guala, king of Mumu, was killed by the Norsemen.


Sechannán son of Conaing, king of Carraic Brachaidi, dies.

# 860 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 859.


Mael Sechnaill, king of Temair, led an army of the Laigin and Mumu and Connacht
and the southern Uí Néill into the North, and he halted at Mag Dumai near Ard
Macha. Aed son of Niall and Flann son of Conaing attacked their camp by night
and killed some people in the middle of the camp; and Aed was subsequently
overcome and left many dead behind him, Mael Sechnaill's army holding to its
position.


Aed son of Dub dá Bairenn, king of Uí Fhidgeinte, dies.




Flannacán son of Colmán dies.


Niall son of Iallán, who suffered from paralysis for thirty-three years and was
disturbed by frequent visions both false and true, rested in Christ.

# 861 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 860.


Mide was invaded by Aed son of Niall with foreigners.


Gormlaith, daughter of Donnchad, a most charming queen of the Irish, died after
repentance.

# 862 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 861.


Domnall son of Ailpín, king of the Picts, died.


Aed son of Niall went with (?) the kings of the foreigners into Mide, and
plundered Mide with Flann son of Conaing.


Aed son of Niall begins to reign.


Finán of Cluain Chaín, a bishop and an anchorite, and Muirgius, anchorite of
Ard Macha, ended their lives in peace.


Mael Sechnaill son of Mael Ruanaid son of Donnchad son of Domnall son of
Murchad of Mide son of Diarmait the Harsh son of Airmedach the One-eyed son of
Conall of the Sweet Voice, son of Suibne son of Colmán the great son of
Diarmait the red son of Fergus Wrymouth, king of all Ireland, died on the third
feria, the second of the Kalends of December 30 Nov., in the 16th year of his
reign.


Ruarc son of Braen, king of Uí Dúnlainge, was slain.


Maelodar grandson of Tinnred, an eminent Irish leech, dies.

# 863 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 862.


Aed son of Cumuscach, king of Uí Nialláin, died.


Muiredach son of Mael Dúin, prior of Ard Macha and king of Int Airthir, was
killed by Domnall son of Aed son of Niall.


Muirecán son of Diarmait, king of Nás and eastern Life, was killed by the
Norsemen.


The caves of Achad Aldai, and of Cnodba, and of Boadán's Mound above Dubad, and
of Óengoba's wife, were searched by the foreigners something which had never
been done before. This was the occasion when three kings of the foreigners,
i.e. Amlaíb and Ímar and Auisle, plundered the land of Flann son of Conaing;
and Lorcán son of Cathal, king of Mide, was with them in this.



# 864 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 863.


Lorcán son of Cathal, king of Mide, was blinded by Aed son of Niall, king of
Temair.


Conchobor son of Donnchad, one of two kings of Mide, was put to death in water
at Cluain Iraird by Amlaíb, king of the foreigners.


Aed son of Niall and Flann son of Conaing gained a great victory over Ainbíth
son of Aed and the Ulaid in the territory of the descendants of Conall the
Craftsman.


Muiredach son of Niall, abbot of Lugbad and other churches, dies.


Aedgein the Briton, bishop of Cell Dara, scribe, anchorite and an elder almost
116 years of age, rested.

# 865 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 864.


A solar eclipse on the Kalends 1st of January, and a lunar eclipse in the same
month.


Cellach son of Ailill, abbot of Cell Dara and Í, fell asleep in the country of
the Picts.


Tigernach son of Fócarta, king of Loch Gabor and one of two kings of Brega,
died.


The Britons were driven from their land by the Saxons and were placed in
bondage in Móin Chonáin.


Tadc son of Diarmait, king of the Uí Cheinnselaig, was killed by his own
kinsmen and his people.


Conmal, steward of Tamlacht, and Tuathal son of Artgus, chief bishop of
Foirtriu and abbot of Dún Caillen, fell asleep.

# 866 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 865.


Amlaíb and Auisle went with the foreigners of Ireland and Scotland to Fortriu,
plundered the entire Pictish country and took away hostages from them.


Colcu and Aed, two abbots of Mainister Buiti, died in the same year.


Cernachán son of Cumuscach, king of Ráith Airthir, was treacherously killed by
Móracán son of Aedacán.


Aed son of Niall plundered all the strongholds of the foreigners i.e. in the
territory of the North, both in Cenél Eógain and Dál Araidi, and took away
their heads, their flocks, and their herds from camp by battle (?). A victory
was gained over them at Loch Febail and twelve score heads taken thereby.




Loch Léibinn was turned to blood, which gave rise to clots of blood like little
lungs around its edge.

# 867 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 866.


Mael Dúin son of Aed, king of Ailech, died in religion after long suffering.


Robartach of Finnglas, bishop and scribe, and Conall bishop of Cell Scíre, and
Coscrach of Tech Taille, a scribe, and Oegedchar, abbot of Condaire and Lann
Ela, and Cormac grandson of Liathán, scribe and bishop and anchorite all fell
asleep in Christ.


Mael Tuile, abbot of Ára Airthir, rested.


Guaire son of Dub dá Bairenn dies.


Abán son of Cinaed, heir designate of Connacht, was killed with fire by
Sochlachán son of Diarmait.


Auisle, one of three kings of the heathens, was killed by his kinsmen in guile
and parricide.


The dark foreigners won a battle over the northern Saxons at York, in which
fell Aelle, king of the northern Saxons.


Amlaíb's fort at Cluain Dolcáin was burned by Gaíthíne's son and Mael Ciaráin
son of Rónán, and the aforesaid commanders caused a slaughter of a hundred of
the leaders of the foreigners in the vicinity of Cluain Dolcáin on the same
day.


Muiredach son of Cathal, king of Uí Chremthainn, died of prolonged paralysis.

# 868 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 867.


Cellach son of Cumuscach, abbot of Fobar, a learned and most talented young
man, died.


Connmach, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, fell asleep in Christ on the night of the
Kalends 1st of January.


Daniél, abbot of Glenn dá Locha and Tamlacht, and Caemán son of Dálach, abbot
of Dam Liac, died.


Aed son of Niall won a battle at Cell Ua nDaigri against the Uí Néill of Brega,
and the Laigin, and a large force of the foreigners, i.e. three hundred or
more; and Flann son of Conaing, king of all Brega, and Diarmait son of
Etarscéle, king of Loch Gabor, fell therein; and in this battle very many of
the heathens were slaughtered; and


Fachtna son of Mael Dúin, heir designate of the North, and many others, fell in
the counter-attack of the battle.

         1. (Flann son of Conaing, a king until now,
            Took the land once held by Tadc son of Cian;
            He was a golden shoot to protect the seed of Niall
            That had sprouted from the fairy hill of good Cerna.
         2. It is strange to look at the estuary of the Boyne
            (It is fitting to keep a pact in mind)
            And not to see the active(?) warrior of Fernach(?)
            Flann of populous Bregmag.)


Congal son of Fedach, abbot of Cell Delga, a scribe, rested.


A remarkable eruption of water from Sliab Cualann, with little black fishes.


A great windstorm on the Feast of Martin 11 Nov.


Rechtabra son of Murchad, abbot of Corcach Mór, fell asleep.

# 869 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 868.


Martan, abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis and Daiminis, a scribe, and Niallán, bishop
of Sláine, fell asleep.


Cormac son of Éládach, abbot of Saiger, bishop and scribe, ended his life as a
very old man.


Flann son of Ferchar, steward of Ard Macha and superior of Lann Léire, ended
alas! a short life.


Mael Ciaráin son of Rónán, royal champion of eastern Ireland, a warrior who
plundered the foreigners, was killed.


Cernach son of Eochu, chief of Mugdorna of Brega, and Ruadachán son of Niall,
chief of Uí Fhorannán, died.


Ard Macha was plundered by Amlaíb and burned with its oratories. Ten hundred
were carried off or killed, and great rapine also committed.


Donnacán son of Cétfaid, king of Uí Cheinnselaig, was treacherously killed by
his companion.


Ailill of Clochar, scribe, bishop and abbot of Clochar Mac nDaiméni, fell
asleep.


Dubthach son of Mael Tuile, most learned of the Latinists of all Europe, fell
asleep in Christ.

U869.10

Mael Brigte son of Spelán, king of Conaille, died in clerical life.



# 870 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 869.


Suairlech of Int Eidnén, bishop and anchorite and abbot of Cluain Iraird, the
best exponent of religion in all Ireland, rested.


Aed son of Niall overran Laigin from Áth Cliath to Gabruán. Cerball son of
Dúngal with what force he could gather invaded them and reached Dún Bolg. The
Laigin attacked Cerball's encampment and slew Gaíthíne's son and others, and
were then thrown back in flight with their king i.e. Muiredach son of Bran, and
some of them were slaughtered.


Dálach son of Muirchertach, chief of Cenél Conaill, was killed by his own
people.


Diarmait son of Diarmait killed a man in Ard Macha in front of the door of the
house of Aed, king of Temair.


Dub dá Thuile, abbot of Liath Mór Mo-Chaemóc, and the anchorite Maelodar, abbot
of Daiminis, and the abbot of Ciarán's hermitage of Belach Dúin, i.e. Cumscuth,
a scribe and a bishop; Comgán the Tall, anchorite of Tamlacht and disciple of
Mael Ruain; Connla, anchorite of Druim Cara of Ard Ciannacht all ended their
lives in Christ.


The siege of Ail Cluaithe by the Norsemen: Amlaíb and Ímar, two kings of the
Norsemen, laid siege to the fortress and at the end of four months they
destroyed and plundered it.


Mael Sechnaill son of Niall, one of the two kings of southern Brega, was
treacherously killed by Ulf the dark foreigner.


Cobthach son of Muiredach, superior of Cell Dara, fell asleep.

# 871 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 870.


Cathalán son of Indrechtach, one of two kings of Ulaid, was deceitfully killed
at the instigation of Aed.


Amlaíb and Ímar returned to Áth Cliath from Alba with two hundred ships,
bringing away with them in captivity to Ireland a great prey of Angles and
Britons and Picts.


The storming of Dún Sobairche, which had never been achieved before: the
foreigners were at it with the Cenél Eógain.


Ailill son of Dúnlang, king of Laigin, was killed by the Norsemen.




Bishop Ailill, abbot of Fobar, fell asleep in Christ.


Cú Raí son of Aldnia, of Inis Clothrann and of Fochla of Mide, a learned abbot
and the most expert in the histories of the Irish, fell asleep in Christ.


Colgu son of Mael Tuile, priest, anchorite and abbot of Cluain Chonaire Tómáin,
rested.


The pilgrim Maengal, abbot of Bennchor, happily ended his life as an old man.


Mael Mide son of Cumuscach, prior of Cluain Moccu Nóis, dies.

# 872 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 871.


Gnia, superior of Dam Liac, an anchorite, bishop and excellent scribe, died.


Mael Ruanaid, son of Mael Curarda, chief of Uí Moccu Uais of the North, died.


Cenn Faelad grandson of Mochtigern, king of Caisel, rested in peace after
prolonged suffering.


Ferdomnach, superior of Cluain Moccu Nóis, fell asleep.


Artgal, king of the Britons of Strathclyde, was killed at the instigation of
Constantine son of Cinaed.


Bishop Mael Tuile, superior of Tuilén, fell asleep in Christ.


Loingsech son of Foillén, superior of Cell Ausile, dies.


Robartach of Dairmag, an excellent scribe, rested.


Mugrón son of Mael Cothaid, one of two kings of Connacht, died.

# 873 A.D.

Kalends of January twenty-seventh of the moon. AD 872.


Flaithbertach son of Dub Roip, king of Corcu Mruad Ninais, Uathmarán son of
Brócán, king of Uí Fhiachrach Aidni, and Dúnadach son of Rogallach, king of
Cenél Cairpri Móir, die.


Lethlabar son of Loingsech, king of the Province, ended his life as an old man.


Ímar, king of the Norsemen of all Ireland and Britain, ended his life.


Dúngal son of Maenach, superior of Inis Caín Dega, rested in peace.




Donn Cuan son of Flannacán, was treacherously killed by Conaing son of Flann.


The fair of Tailtiu was not held, although there was no just and worthy reason
for this something which we have not known to happen from ancient times.


Colmán, bishop, scribe and abbot of Naendruim, rested.


Flaithbertach son of Muirchertach, superior of Dún Caillen, died.

# 874 A.D.

Kalends of January ninth of the moon
[...]
.AD 873.


Aed son of Fiangus, superior of Ros Comáin, a bishop and an excellent scribe,
and Mael Mórda son of Diarmait, a bishop and scribe, died.


Torpaid, superior of Tamlacht, a bishop and excellent scribe, fell asleep in
Christ.


Bishop Féthgna, successor of Patrick and head of religion in all Ireland,
rested in peace on the day before the Nones 6th of October.


Aed son of Niall led an army to Laigin and they profaned Cell Ausili, and other
churches were burned with their oratories.


Cell Mór of Mag Enir was plundered by the foreigners.

# 875 A.D.

Kalends of January twentieth of the moon
[...]
. AD 874.


Maengal, tanist of Cluain Moccu Nóis, and Robartach son of the Artificer,
bishop of Cell Dara and an excellent scribe and the superior of Cell Achaid,
and Lachtnán son of Mochtigern, bishop of Cell Dara and superior of Ferna, died
.


Muiredach son of Bran, with the army of the Laigin, laid waste the country as
far as Sliab Monduirn and returned to his own territory before evening.


The Picts encountered the dark foreigners in battle, and a great slaughter of
the Picts resulted.


Oistín son of Amlaíb, king of the Norsemen, was deceitfully killed by Albann.


Mac Oige, superior of Tamlacht, and Mael Bennachta, bishop of Lusca, fell
asleep in peace.


Fechtnach, abbot of Glenn dá Locha, died.

# 876 A.D.

Kalends of January first of the moon
[...]
. AD 875.


Constantine son of Cinaed, king of the Picts, Cinaed


abbot of Achad Bó Cainnig, Congalach son of Fínnechta, king of the Airgialla,
and Fedach, superior of Dísert Diarmata, die.


Cairpre son of Diarmait, king of Uí Cheinnselaig, was killed by his own
kinsmen.


The fair of Tailtiu was not held, although there was no just and worthy reason
for this.


Domnall, bishop of Corcach, an excellent scribe, died suddenly.

# 877 A.D.

Kalends of January twelfth of the moon
[...]
. AD 876.


Eógan and Mael Tuile, grandson of Cuanu, two abbots of Cluain Moccu Nóis, fell
asleep in peace.


Donnchad son of Aedacán son of Conchobor, was deceitfully killed by Flann son
of Mael Sechnaill.


Rhodri son of Merfyn, king of the Britons, came in flight from the dark
foreigners to Ireland.


Mael Brigte, bishop of Sláine, fell asleep in peace.


A skirmish at Loch Cuan between the fair heathens and the dark heathens, in
which Albann, king of the dark heathens, fell.


Sochartach son of Brócán, chief of Uí Chormaic, died.


A change of abbots in Ard Macha, i.e. Ainmire instead of Mael Coba.


Cathalán son of Cernach, king of Fir Chúl, dies.

# 878 A.D.

Kalends of January twenty-third of the moon
[...]
. AD 877.


Rhodri son of Merfyn, king of the Britons, was killed by the Saxons.


Aed son of Cinaed, king of the Picts, was killed by his own associates.


Gairbíth son of Mael Brigte, king of Conaille, was beheaded by the Uí Echach.

         1. (Rhodri of Manu, splendid here,
            Aed from the lands of Cenn Tíre,
            Donnchad, lustrous stuff of sovereignty,
            Gairíth, diadem of smooth Macha
         2. Whenever I bring it to mind
            It inflames the limits of my heart,
            Cold flags over temples of the buried,
            Barrfhinn of Bile is distraught.)




Cumuscach son of Muiredach, king of Uí Chremthainn, was killed by the Ulaid.


Mael Pátraic son of Cellach, superior of Mainister Buiti, died suddenly.


Great wind and lightning. A shower of blood fell and clots of gore and blood
were found in the fields.


The fair of Tailtiu was not held, although there was no just and worthy reason
for this.


There was a lunar eclipse on the Ides 15th of October, the fourteenth of the
moon, on the fourth feria about the third watch; and a solar eclipse on the
fourth of the Kalends of November 29 October, the twenty-eighth of the moon, on
the fourth feria, about the seventh hour of daylight, fifteen solar days having
intervened.


The shrine of Colum Cille and his other halidoms arrived in Ireland, having
been taken in flight to escape the foreigners.

# 879 A.D.

Kalends of January fourth of the moon
[...]
. AD 878.


Aed son of Niall, king of Temair, fell asleep on the twelfth of the Kalends of
December 20 Nov. at Druim Inasclainn in the territory of Conaille.

         1. (Twelve days before the melodious Kalends
            Of December a harsh company
            A wonderful person died to your loss(?),
            Aed of Ailech, over-king of the Irish.
         2. A generous prudent man of shields
            Who brought plenty to landed Temair,
            Against iron-tipped spears a buckler
            From the forge-fire of the land of the sons of Mil.)


(Flann son of Mael Sechnaill begins to reign.)


Bishop Tigernach son of Muiredach, superior of Druim Inasclainn, rested after
prolonged suffering.


Fergil son of Cumsad, abbot of Domnach Sechnaill, was secretly murdered.


Aengus son of Cinaed, chief of the people of Ard Ciannachta, dies.


Mael Coba son of Crunnmael, superior of Ard Macha, and the lector i.e. Mochta,
were taken prisoner by the foreigners.


Great scarcity of food for cattle in the spring. Great profusion in the autumn.




Mael Caere, chief of Uí Chremthainn, was killed.


Ualgarg son of Flaithbertach, heir designate of the North, dies.

U879.10

Finnechta son of Mael Corcra, king of the Luigni of Connacht, dies.


Ainmire, superior of Ard Macha for nine months, fell asleep.


Dúngal, superior of Lethglenn, dies.

# 880 A.D.

Kalends of January fifteenth of the moon
[...]
. AD. 879.


Feradach son of Cormac, abbot of Í, rested.


Mael Ciaráin son of Conaing, king of Tethba, ended his life as an old man in
religion.


Duiblitir, superior of Cluain Eóis and Tech Airennáin, died.


Muirecán son of Cormaic, superior of Sentrabh, dies.


Mael Mithig, son of Dub Indrecht, was killed.

# 881 A.D.

Kalends of January sixth of the moon
[...]
. AD 880.


Ferchar, abbot of Bennchor, died.


Crunnmael of Cluain Chaín, a bishop and anchorite, fell asleep.


The oratory of Cianán was destroyed by the foreigners, and many people were
taken from it. Afterwards Barith, a great despot of the Norsemen, was killed by
St. Ciannán.


Mael Sinchill son of Mugrón, king of Uí Fhailgi, dies.


Aengus son of Mael Curarda, superior of Ard Sratha, Aenacán son of Ruaidrí,
superior of Lusca, and Flaithemán son of Cellach, king of Uí Briúin of Cualu,
die.


Suibne, bishop of Cell Dara, rested.


Bishop Ruidgel, abbot of Imlech Ibuir, rested.


Mael Fábaill son of Loingsech, king of Carraic Brachaide, dies.

# 882 A.D.

Kalends of January seventh of the moon
[...]
. AD 881.


Flann son of Mael Sechnaill led an army both of foreigners and Irish into the
North. He camped at Mag eter dí Glais, and Ard Macha was invaded by him.


Muirchertach son of Niall, abbot of Daire Calgaig, and other monasteries,
rested.




Lorcán son of Coscrach, king of Uí Nialláin, and Donnacán son of Fogartach,
king of Fernmag, fell by one another.


A skirmish between the Conaille of Muirtheimne and the Maid, in which fell
Ainfíth son of Aed king of Ulaid, and Conallán son of Mael Dúin, king of Cuib,
and other nobles. The Conaille were victors.


Scannlán, superior of Dún Lethglaisi, was killed by the Ulaid.


Cormac son of Ciarán, prior of Cluain Ferta Brénainn and superior of Tuaim dá
Gualann, dies.


Conchobor son of Tadc, king of the three Connachta, ended his life as an old
man.


Aedán, superior of Cluain Iraird, rested in peace.


Dub Inse, superior of Inis Caín Dega, dies.

# 883 A.D.

Kalends of January eighteenth of the moon. AD 882.


Mael Ruáin, bishop of Lusca, fell asleep in peace.


Cumuscach son of Domnall, king of Cenél Loegaire, dies.


Braen son of Tigernach was killed by Ainfíth son of Gairbíth.

         1. (Braen son of Tigernach the guileless
            Who had a fine reputation in this world,
            That Aengus should be slain like Braen(?),
            Is it not one of the afflictions of God?)


Death of Auisle's son at the hands of Iergne's son and the daughter of Mael
Sechnaill.


Ainfíth son of Mugrón, king of the Mugdorna of Brega, was killed.


Eochucán son of Aed, one of two kings of Ulaid, was killed by the sons of
Ainfíth son of Aed.


Cathasach son of Robartach, superior of Ard Macha, rested in peace.


Aengus son of Mael Dúin, heir designate of the North, was beheaded by the Dál
Araidi.



# 884 A.D.

Kalends of January twenty-ninth of the moon
[...]
.AD 883.


Ailbrenn son of Maicthech, superior of Cluain Iraird, fell asleep after a
prolonged illness.


Suairlech, superior of Ard Brecáin, ended his life as an old man.


Domnall son of Muirecán, king of Laigin, was killed by his associates.


Cairpre son of Dúnlang, king of western Life, died.


Conaing son of Flann, heir designate of Cianacht, was beheaded by the Laigin.


Donn Cuan son of Condálach, king of Cianacht of Glenn Geimin, dies.


Dúnacán son of Tuathchar, king of Gailenga of Collumair, was killed by the
Gailenga Móra.


Cormac son of Ceithernach, prior of Tír dá Glas and Cluain Ferta Brénainn,
rested.


Rogaillnech, abbot of Bennchor, Dúnacán son of Cormac, abbot of Mainister
Buiti, and Conallán son of Mael Teimin, superior of Inis Caín Dega, fell
asleep.

# 885 A.D.

Kalends of January tenth of the moon
[...]
. AD 884.


Tuileflaith, abbess of Cell Dara, fell asleep; and Scannal, bishop of Cell
Dara, dies.


Domnall son of Cinaed, king of Cenél Laegaire, died in clerical life.


Mael Tuile son of Fechtnach, superior of Glas Noíden, dies.


Mael Pátraic son of Mael Curarda, king of the Airgialla, was killed by his
associates.


A solar eclipse, and stars were seen in the heavens.


Mael Dúin son of Aengus, king of Caille Follamhain, dies.


Cormac, superior of Cluain Iraird and bishop of Dam Liac, rests after prolonged
illness.


A child at Craeb Laisre spoke two months after its birth, which had not been
heard of from ancient times.


Muiredach son of Bran, king of Laigin and superior of Cell Dara, fell asleep.

U885.10

Secret murder was committed in Cell Dara.


Mugrón son of Cenn Faelad, superior of Cluain Ferta Brénainn, dies.



# 886 A.D.

Kalends of January twenty-first of the moon.
[...]
. AD 885.


Éiremón son of Aed, one of two kings of Ulaid, was killed by Eolóir son of I
ergne.


Clothobar son of Mael Tuile, prior of Cluain Iraird, and Robartach son of
Colcu, superior of Cell Tuama, fell asleep.


Fiachna son of Ainfíth, king of Ulaid, was killed by his associates.


Scannal son of Fergil, superior of Domnach Sechnaill, was killed by his own
kinsmen.

# 887 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 886.


Murchad son of Mael Dúin, heir designate of the North, was killed by Flannacán
son of Fogartach, king of Fernmag.


Tigernach son of Tolarg, heir designate of southern Brega, was killed by his
associates.


A letter, with the 'Law of Sunday' and other good instructions, came to Ireland
with the Pilgrim.


Bishop Echaid of Lann, Comgán's son, ended his life as an old man.


Mael Muru, chief poet of Ireland, died.

         1. (The choice earth has not covered,
            To Temair's multitudes there shall not come,
            Ireland of the great territories(?) shall not contain
            A man like the pure and gentle Mael Muru.
         2. There has never tasted death fearlessly,
            Nor reached the known dead,
            The cultivator's soil has never covered
            A more wonderful keeper of tradition.)

# 888 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 887.


Mael Coba son of Crunnmael abbot of Ard Macha, ended his life as an old man.


Mael Tuile son of Cilén, superior of Cluain Ferta Brénainn, rested.


Mael Pátraic, scribe and excellent scholar, superior of Treóit and steward of
the community of Patrick for the district south of the Mountain, rested.


Dúnchad son of Dub dá Bairenn, king of Caisel, dies.




The foreigners inflicted a battle-rout on Flann son of Mael Sechnaill and there
fell there Aed son of Conchobor, king of Connacht, and Lergus son of Cruinnén
bishop of Cell Dara, and Donnchad son of Mael Dúin, superior of Cell Delca and
other monasteries. The battle of the Pilgrim.


Cerball son of Dúngal, king of Osraige, died suddenly.


Cú cen Máthair, superior of Imlech Ibuir, rested.


Tolarg son of Cellach, one of two kings of southern Brega, ended his life as an
old man.


Sigfrith son of Ímar, king of the Norsemen, was deceitfully killed by his
kinsman.

U888.10

It happened that the fair of Tailtiu was not held.

# 889 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 888.


An expedition by Domnall son of Aed with the men of the north of Ireland
against the southern Uí Néill.


Mael Martain, successor of Cainnech, died.


Maenach, superior of Cell Achaid Droma Fota, died.


The fair of Tailtiu was not held.

# 890 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 889.


The sky appeared to be on fire on the night of the Kalends 1st of January.


Mael Pátraic son of Niall, superior of Sláine, fell asleep happily.


Eógan son of Cenn Faelad, superior of Imlech Ibuir, was killed.


Giblechán son of Mael Brigte, king of the Conaille of Muirtheimne, dies.


Flann daughter of Dúngal, queen of the king of Temair, fell asleep in penance.


Airmedach, superior of Mag Bile, fell asleep.

# 891 A.D.

Kalends of January
[...]
. AD 890 alias 891.


Flann son of Mael Dúin, abbot of Í, rested in peace.


Conchobor son of Flannacán, king of Uí Fhailgi, was destroyed by fire in Cluain
Fhata. The community (?) of Finnia was outraged in the church, and Finnia's
halidoms were profaned by him and burned.




Mael Mórda son of Gairbíth i.e. king of Conaille of Muirtheimne, was beheaded
by Cellach son of Flannacán.


Cormac, superior of Fobar and tanist-abbot of Cluain Moccu Nóis, dies.


Cormac son of Fianamail, superior of Druim Inasclainn, fell asleep.


Sechnasach, bishop of Lusca, fell asleep.


Fothad, superior of Mainister Buiti, dies.
